# Divergences from the original

Where "Proving Grounds for Meitou" differs from Shutori's RE_Kenshi plugin 1.3.4 (upstream commit `4a20d48`), and the original
bugs it keeps on purpose. Each task that causes a difference adds it here: what, why, effect on play.

A player-visible string of the original (`fidelity/strings.json`) that the port does not reproduce verbatim gets a line of its own
starting `string:`, then the text, then the reason, for example `string: Reading — sidecar file IO is dropped`. The strings gate
(`Tests/Fidelity/StringsTests.cs`, a hard gate from task 9.3) accepts such a line in place of the text in the C# source.

## Forced by Meitou

From `00-overview-and-rules.md` §1.

| Original | In this port | Why |
|---|---|---|
| 37 KenshiLib hooks on Kenshi.exe | Meitou public API + Harmony patches (CAP table "The original's hooks") | different engine |
| One per-frame UI hook (`ForgottenGUI::update`), real-time milliseconds (`GetTickCount`, dt capped at 1 s) | One tick system; a clock counting simulation time (1 tick = 1/30 s, `PgClock`), skipped while paused like the original's paused checks. Each world's clock starts at 3,600,000 ms (one hour, `SimClock.StartTicks`), not 0, and wraps at 2^32 ms like a `DWORD` (after about 49.7 days of play in one world) | determinism, saves; identical at 1x speed. At 2x/5x the cadences follow game time (the ring guard no longer gets "aggressive above x5"). The start offset keeps the uptime clock's behaviour: a throttle against a never-set stamp of 0 (`now - g_last >= interval`) fires on the first frame as in the original, and a stamp of 0 still means "never"; one hour exceeds every GetTickCount() interval of the C++ (largest: 90 s, ArenaIngress.cpp:98) |
| Sidecar files `proving_grounds_mmr.json` + backups beside the save, generation keys, emergency-save search | The same v16 JSON snapshot stored inside the save as Meitou mod data (`IModSaveData`) | Meitou saves mod data with the save, so the sidecar mismatch bugs cannot happen. `Core/LeaderboardSavePolicy.cs` still ports the sidecar rules (owner key, fork save, accept/write) line for line; nothing calls them |
| Fighter identity: a UUID written into each character's save state (`FighterIdentity.cpp`, hooks on `Character::serialise` / `loadFromSerialise` / `_DESTRUCTOR`) | Identity from PortraitSerial+RecordId instead of a UUID in the character's save state: token `<PortraitSerial as %08x, lowercase>-<RecordId>` (`Core/Glue/FighterIdentityMap.cs`, `src/Game/FighterIdentity.cs`, task 2.3); "create" registers the token in the world's list (saved by task 2.4); the load/destroy hooks become a lazy scan of the character table in `SlowWorld` (only when its spawn counter or count moved). Same rules otherwise: `Find` answers only a created token, two live characters with one created token block both careers for the world (same log texts). No serial (or 0) = no identity, the fighter is skipped as the original skips one without an id. No hook health: `CanPersist` is always true and the "hooks ready" / "hook installation failed" lines are gone | Meitou has no per-character mod save slot; PortraitSerial survives squad moves, recruiting, save and load (live: `tools/scenarios/identity.sh`). Differences: two live characters with the same token share a career key and trip the duplicate guard where the original's UUIDs would not. How a serial is made: `PortraitSerial ??= (uint)(Rng.Float(Rng.Mix(Rng.Key(new CharacterId(slot, generation)) ^ (ulong)tick * 0x9E3779B97F4A7C15UL)) * 4e9f)` (CharacterTable.cs:1488; Key = slot | generation << 32, Rng.cs:181; Float keeps 24 bits, Rng.cs:187): no world or save seed goes in (World.Seed exists, World.cs:261, but is not used here), a character loaded from a save keeps its saved serial (SavedPopulation.cs:548-551, before Spawn's `??=`), and `World.Tick` starts at 0 in every world (World.cs:421 private set, only `Tick++` at :636), so after a load a NEW character spawned in the same slot, generation and tick as a pre-load character's own spawn gets the same serial, and with the same RecordId (a respawning town template) the same token. Measured live (2026-11-12, `tools/scenarios/identity.sh`, `pg identity-dupes`, Scratch Test, 60 s at speed 5 before and after a quicksave + load): 371 / 376 characters before the load, 366 / 384 after (19 made after the load), 0 tokens held twice at each of the 4 points and 0 post-load new characters sharing a pre-load token. Within one world a repeat needs a 24-bit hash clash plus the same RecordId; the same new-game start gives the same serials in every new world (tokens are per save, so harmless). A larger or longer world was not measured. A prisoner whose save state was older keeps its token (the original's residual "second row" risk, PA §2.16, cannot happen) |
| The sidecar `proving_grounds_mmr.json` holds only the snapshot JSON; the identity UUIDs sit in each character's save state | The save data of the system (`IModSaveData`, task 2.4, `Core/Glue/ArenaSaveData.cs`): byte `0x01` (format version), the snapshot JSON byte for byte as the codec writes it, a NUL, then the mod-side extension `{"fighterIdentities":[...]}` with the created identity tokens in ordinal order | the port has no per-character slot for the token, and the standings rows do not carry every created token (a match creates ids for all its fighters before it skips some, LeaderboardStore.cpp:254-273); the NUL cannot occur in the JSON (the codec rejects it), so the snapshot JSON stays unchanged |
| `saveKey` = the save folder name, `generation` = a UUID written into the native save's CAMERA record, both matched on load (ArenaNativePersistence.cpp:211, 243-256, 336-348) | `saveKey` = the fixed `"meitou"` (`ArenaSaveData.InSaveKey`), `generation` = a new GUID per save; nothing is matched on load | Meitou keeps the data inside the save. The plan asked for `saveKey = ""`, but the codec requires a nonempty one (ArenaSnapshotJson.cpp:365 `Text(v,"saveKey","$",true)`), so "" cannot be written |
| Unreadable or mismatched sidecar (ArenaNativePersistence.cpp:387-395, LeaderboardStore.cpp:164-170): the ledger is blocked (`g_ledger.Block()`, `HasActiveSave` false): ratings, marks, rewards and the town schedule refuse; a save reports "Arena portion of save unavailable: <reason>..." and publishes nothing, so the old sidecar stays; the player sees "Arena progress blocked: <sidecar path> <field>: <message>. Preserve the file/backups or use the leaderboard's explicit reset..."; the explicit reset is offered only for WrongSnapshot (:389-393), not for bad data | The same for unreadable save data (bad frame, InvalidJson, InvalidData, UnsupportedVersion, or any exception while reading: reason `read failed: <Type>: <message>`, fail closed; `Core/Glue/ArenaWorldState.cs`): blocked ledger, the same texts byte for byte, the setters and the save refuse, no reset offered, and the save keeps the arena data it had (Write throws on purpose; Meitou keeps the mod's old file, ModHost.cs:410-416, SaveCapture.cs:86, SaveGame.cs:285). Differences: the path in the report is the recovered copy `<mod folder>/recovered/<utc yyyyMMdd-HHmmss>.dat` (the whole payload as read, written at load through a `.tmp` file; the plan said `.json`, but it is not a JSON file) instead of the sidecar's, or "the saved arena data" when that copy could not be written; an extra log line `PG: saved arena data unreadable: <reason>` (the plan's); a framing failure (empty, wrong version byte, no NUL, bad identity list) has no codec field, so its reason is a plain text; a WrongSnapshot cannot happen (nothing is matched), so the reset is never offered | the data lives inside the save; the file path is the only thing Meitou changes |
| Legacy snapshot (schema < 11): a fresh ledger (default `Snapshot`, card seed 1), the report "Legacy arena progress reset. Original JSON will be backed up when this slot is next saved.", the original backed up by the next publish (ArenaNativePersistence.cpp:381-385, ArenaSnapshotRepository.cpp:163-176) | The same, with the original bytes written to `recovered/` by the next save (Write); when that copy fails, the save reports "Arena portion of save FAILED: Could not back up the legacy arena snapshot. ..." and keeps the old bytes, and the next save retries (as the C++ publish fails with its backup, ArenaSnapshotRepository.cpp:175-176), so the report is true word for word | the backup lives in recovered/ instead of beside the sidecar; only an imported RE_Kenshi sidecar (optional task 9.7) can be legacy |
| A save whose arena part cannot be captured (blocked ledger, a reserved purchase) or encoded reports "Arena portion of save unavailable: ..." / "Arena portion of save FAILED at <sidecar>: ...", publishes no sidecar (the old one stays) and keeps the live progress (ArenaNativePersistence.cpp:205-210, 173-196) | The same texts (the FAILED one without " at <sidecar path>"); `Write` throws `ArenaDataNotWritten` on purpose so Meitou keeps the arena data the save had (ModHost.cs:410-416; the capture base is a copy of the loaded save, SaveCapture.cs:86, SaveGame.cs:285); Meitou also prints "mod ProvingGroundsMeitou: save data not written: ..." to stderr | no separate file to leave alone: throwing is Meitou's "keep the old data" path |
| A Meitou import of a save | Meitou's import does not carry mod data (CAP 9.1): a fresh ledger (the C++ `PrepareImportedSnapshot` keeps progression) | no mod data to import; RE_Kenshi sidecars are optional task 9.7 |
| New game: `CompleteWorldTransition(true)` after the NEWGAME dispatch activates a fresh ledger with card seed `GetTickCount() \| 1u` (WorldLifecycle.cpp:216-225, LeaderboardStore.cpp:194-200); a load without a sidecar uses a default `Snapshot` (seed 1, ArenaNativePersistence.cpp:381-384) | The first serial step of the world decides: `World.Saved` null (a world that started new, World.cs:321-324) gets the new-game seed, a world loaded from a save (a save made without the mod, an import) keeps the seed-1 default. A save made before that first step writes the default (seed 1) | Meitou has no new-game callback for a mod system; `World.Saved` is set only by `SaveLoader.Load` (SaveLoader.cs:241) |
| Save safety runs inside Kenshi's SaveManager hooks on one thread: a manual save during a match cancels it and saves in the same call; an autosave while busy is run by the updateAutoSave hook when idle (while Kenshi's autosave timer is not paused); a queued SAVEGAME is held by the execute hook (WorldLifecycle.cpp:84-226) | One bool prefix on `GameSaves.QuickSaveNow` (every Meitou save) decides on the host's frame thread from a state the sim thread publishes each tick. A manual save that needs preparation (a match, ingress, prisoners, town activity, a town booking, a limb shop visit, a cage-return failure) is refused at once (the C++ notice "Proving Grounds match cancelled to safely save the game." shown at the key press when the arena is busy and the C++ would show it first; a double press shows it once and prepares once; while waiting for cage locks or after the unsecured-return warning the sim shows the messages in the C++ order), the C++ preparation runs in the next tick, and the system then re-requests the same save (`SaveRequestedFolder` / `QuickSaveRequested`, set in a `HandleSaves` prefix), which goes through. A delayed autosave is released by a tick (ticks run only while the game is not paused). A re-requested save is held while save-sensitive (the execute hook). Every message shows through Player.Notice(text) as Meitou shows a world.Post message (Kenshi's `showPlayerAMessage(text, true)` has one channel; Meitou's `error` styling is not used). Design and thread handshake: src/Game/WorldLifecycle.cs header | the save starts on the frame thread, which must not touch the world. Effect: a manual save during a match lands one tick (1/30 s) later; pressed while the game is paused, it is saved only when the game runs again: the key press then shows the C++ "match cancelled" notice when the C++ would show it first, else the port-only text "Save will run when the game resumes." (a refusal that only cancels a town booking or suspends a limb shop visit, or one that waits for cage locks / follows the unsecured-return warning, whose C++ messages the sim shows when it runs). A world change drops the saves the old world still held (one log line), as the C++ AbandonWorldState does. A re-requested save waits while Meitou is still writing a save (GameSaves.Busy) and starts on a later frame, one per frame; none is lost. A save the player names autosave0-2 counts as an autosave (delayed while busy, not cancelling the match) |
| Native code: every function is compiled before the game runs | A background thread below normal priority compiles the mod's methods ahead of their first call (`src/Engine/PgJitWarmup.cs`, the list `Core/Glue/JitWarmupList.cs`: every non-generic method of the two assemblies and the methods of the closed generic types their fields name, e.g. `TownArenaLogic<CharacterId>`; type initializers never), started once per process by the first world's systems (the AddSystem factory, after every code mod's Initialize, so every Harmony patch is in place first, Meitou's rule, GameMods.cs:20-58); one log line "jit warm-up: <n> methods compiled ahead in <s> s"; off with Meitou's `--no-precompile` (task 9.2b) | Meitou runs without tiered compilation (src/Meitou.Game/Meitou.Game.csproj, DECISIONS 19): a method is compiled, fully optimised, on its first call, and the 9.2b measurement found that landing in the tick (23 to 97 ms at a town bout's first planning, staging, fight start and result, 80 to 100 % JIT time, no collection). Meitou warms its own assemblies the same way (src/Meitou.Game/Host/JitWarmup.cs), not the mods'. The compiled code is the code the first call would compile. Side effects: compiling may run a type initializer (the mod's are allocations and reflection lookups) earlier and on the warm-up thread; a first call made while the warm-up compiles that method waits for it; a generic method, or a generic type the fields do not name, still compiles on its first call |
| New game / load / world teardown hooks (`SaveManager::execute` NEWGAME, `GameWorld::_clearAndDestroyGameWorldStuff`) call `WorldLifecycle::AbandonWorldState`, which resets every module (WorldLifecycle.cpp:203-280) | None: Meitou builds a new `ProvingGroundsSystem` (every subsystem, the identity map, the ledger) for each new game and load | nothing carries between worlds by construction |
| Teams 1v1 requires the town AI hook: `TownAftercare::ControlAvailable()` false refuses it ("Teams 1v1 unavailable: bench AI control is not installed", SparSession.cpp:625-628); the bench reserves each waiting fighter's order receiver for its own AI control (`TownAftercare::ReserveBenchOrders`, :229) | `ControlAvailable` is always true (spike S10 passed live, 2026-11-11): a waiting NPC is held with `CharacterCold.Engaged = true` (+ `Ai.Walking = false`, `TaskStart.Stand` once, re-asserted every serial step; release `Engaged = false`, `Ai.Replan = true`); a waiting player character needs nothing reserved (the AI never holds one, AiSystem.cs:366): its Passive + Hold stance and the move back every 2 s when displaced over 8 units keep it (task 3.3, src/Game/PgSparHost.cs, PgEngine.HoldStill / SetBenchStance / MoveTo) | Meitou has no per-receiver AI hook; Engaged is the engine's own "held by an interaction" flag. AGG (setStandingOrder AGG false) has no counterpart, as in FightStarter |
| The bench recall's "moving away" test reads `CharMovement::isCurrentlyMoving()` and `movement->destination` (a 3D point, SparSession.cpp:240-243); `lookatPosition(pos, true)` turns the new Teams 1v1 pair toward each other (:270-271) | Moving = a horizontal velocity (`CharacterHot.Velocity`), the destination = `CharacterHot.Goal` (X/Z) with the character's own height; facing = `CharacterHot.Yaw = atan2(dx, dz)` | Meitou keeps a 2D goal and turns a standing character by its yaw (CAP 5 "Facing a point"); the attack order given right after turns a fighter anyway |
| A Teams 1v1 KO starts the next fighter's walk-in to the duel spot (`ArenaIngress::BeginWalkIn`, SparSession.cpp:375) | Until task 4.3 the walk-in placeholders refuse (`BeginWalkIn` false), so the next fighter is promoted in place: the original's own fallback when a walk-in cannot start (:375-379) | the ingress is M4 (task 4.3); nothing else differs |
| The results window opens 1.0 s after the end (ResultsUI::ScheduleShow, its delay counted in GetTickCount ms); the prisoner return starts (PrisonerUtil::BeginReturnToCages) | The results window waits the same 1.0 s on the sim clock (task 4.6a, "Results and Leaderboard windows" below); the prisoner return is PrisonerUtil's placeholder (no prisoners until M6, no status); the result data is also logged as one port-only PG-SPAR line (test output). Ratings and Marks go into the ledger as the C++ (src/Game/LeaderboardStore.cs, task 5.1) | the sim clock (Meitou's window / simulation split); the prisoners come with M6; the PG-SPAR line is for the scripted live runs |
| A manual save prepares and saves in one call, so no match can start between the cancel and the capture (WorldLifecycle.cpp:84-150) | `SparSession.StartMatch` refuses first while a save the save safety prepared (or a released autosave) is not captured yet, or a manual save waits for cage locks (`WorldLifecycle.SaveInFlight`), with the port-only status "Saving the game: start the match after the save" | the port prepares on the sim thread and the frame thread captures on a later frame (task 2.5); the refusal closes that window for matches |
| A throw part-way through SparSession's start or stop, or in KOWatcher's scan, is not handled (the C++ has no exceptions there) | Port-only exception safety (task 3.3 and its review): a throw after the start made the session active undoes only what the start got to (the combat log's `match_aborted` with the port-only reason `start_failed` once it started; every remembered or engaged fighter disengaged, the bench released, once the fighters were remembered), clears the session with the port-only status "Match start failed: fighters released" and rethrows; in the stop the result block (freeze, ratings, CombatBalanceLog result, results) and the abort log each run under their own try/catch, so the fighters are always disengaged and the session cleared (a rating is never applied twice); in Teams 1v1 each fighter's disengage and each bench release run under their own try/catch; the save abort always completes; NPCs the bench still holds once no match runs are released (each under its own try/catch). A KOWatcher scan that throws 3 times in a row (`KOWatcherLogic.MaxConsecutiveFailures`; a completed scan resets the count) stops the match as `StopInvalid` ("Over: fighter invalid"), so a recurring throw cannot keep fighters in a match forever; the throw still reaches the system's step-failure log. Each failing step is logged once per step name per world and counted in the F10 rows (SparSession "N stop steps failed", FightStarter "N disengage steps failed") | a throw must never leave fighters with Jobs off and the combat stance on, nor stuck in a match |
| The ring guard steers a fighter in the near band (between the near and out radii) with a forced combat waypoint at the return point, `movement->combatMover.setForcedWP(target)`, so they keep fighting (ArenaRingGuard.cpp:130-139) | The fighter is moved inward on its own bearing from the site's centre by 2.5 units x the ring's scale (2.5 at full size, 1.5 on the small arena), never past the return radius (at the shipped full-size radii the step lands between 142.5 and 145), with `CharacterTable.Place(slot, pos, yaw, task)` keeping its facing and current task (`Core/Glue/RingGuardLogic.cs`, `PgEngine.TryPlaceGroundedKeepingTask`, task 3.4); the 1,000 ms gate per kind and the log line "ring guard steering fighter inward (...)" are the C++'s. Beyond out the C++'s teleport (`hasForcedWP = false` + `_setPositionSimple`, :117-129) is the same Place to the point on the bearing at the return radius (Meitou has no forced waypoint to clear) | Meitou has no forced waypoint for a fighter (CAP 5); starting a walk would end the fight (TaskStart.cs:281-283). Place also zeroes the velocity and drops the path; the combat footwork re-paths from there. The step is a position change where the original's waypoint was a walk: a fighter pressed against the edge is nudged rather than steered |
| The ring guard moves any non-eliminated participant outside the ring, a knocked-out one too (an AvB fighter is not eliminated by a KO) | A downed participant (unconscious or dead, KOWatcher's test) or a carried one (`CharacterCold.CarriedBy` set) is never moved and does not stamp the correction gate (port-only count `downedLeft` in `pg ring status` and the F10 row, once per fighter per 1,000 ms it stays so) | a downed or carried body belongs to Meitou's ragdoll (CAP 5; RagdollSystem.Wanted, RagdollSystem.cs:449-451); moving it under the ragdoll was not tested and is not needed to keep a fight inside |
| The ring guard's corrections keep the fighter's own height (`Ogre::Vector3 target(centre.x + rx, pos.y, centre.z + rz)`, ArenaRingGuard.cpp:114-115) and set it there; Kenshi's physics settles the body | The height is the ground under the target spot at the fighter's floor (`IWalkability.TryStand(x, z, y, out ground)`: GroundHeight with the fighter's height as the floor hint, so a correction never changes floor level), and a spot that is not walkable there is not used: that fighter is left this pass, the gate is not stamped for it (another fighter is still corrected) and it is counted (`unwalkableLeft` in `pg ring status` and the F10 row, once per fighter per 1,000 ms). The test-only `pg ring push` uses the same write | Meitou has no physics to resolve a body put inside a wall or off the floor; its own relocations do the same (AnimationSystem.Relocate refuses an unwalkable spot, AnimationSystem.cs:753-754; DebugCommands.Teleport puts the body on GroundHeight, DebugCommands.cs:608) |
| The ring site's centre is the building's position (`site->getPosition()`, ArenaRingGuard.cpp:82); the guard reads only its x and z and keeps the fighter's height | The same (Centre.Y is never the guard's floor hint: each correction takes the fighter's own height). Measured task 4.8 at Scratch's town Arena 183 (centre (-100348.555, 1052.7124, 13281.8125), `pg ring sample near <fighter>` in arena-ingress): the building's origin lies 25-48 units under the ring's floor, so with the centre's height as the hint no spot stands at 100 / 120 / 142.5 / 147.5 out (0 of 36 bearings each), while with a fighter's height (1086-1089) all 36 stand at every radius (ground 1077.5-1100.4). The guard therefore can return a ring-out fighter there on every bearing; only the test-only `pg ring push`, which took only the centre's height, refused all eight tries of arena-ingress (b) (it now falls back to the character's own height, the guard's hint; its first push there then held) | a fact of the building's origin and Meitou's floor-aware `IWalkability.TryStand(x, z, nearY)` (Walkability.cs:69-73: a surface far above the hint does not count); Kenshi's physics had no such hint |
| The ring guard's site is the building the arena code registers when it arms a bout (`ArenaRingGuard::SetArena(site)`, ArenaIngress.cpp:1343, TownArena.cpp:1715); its scale comes from ArenaIdentity's banner / small-arena reading of that building | Until M4 nothing stages a bout at a site, so no site is set and the guard does nothing (as the C++ with no site); the TEST-ONLY `pg ring` sets one (a point or the mean of characters, with a kind). M4 task 4.3 calls `ArenaRingGuard.SetArena(new RingSite(...))` at ingress start with the staged building (centre = its Position, still-there = it exists and is not destroyed, kind = task 4.1's ArenaIdentity); M7 task 7.3 with the town arena | the Registry, the ingress and ArenaIdentity are M4 |
| F8 toggles the debug menu (off by default) | A setting "Debug menu key": Off (default) / O / N / V | F8 is Meitou's screenshot key (CAP 7.10) |
| "PG CONFIG" button injected into Options > Mods | The same 19 settings as a Meitou mod settings group (Esc > Settings > Mods) | Meitou has a native mod settings page |
| Settings page values stored in pg_config.json's `ui_settings` block by SAVE SETTINGS (PGConfig.cpp:989-1079, splice + `.tmp` + MoveFileExW); read back by Load as the file's last section (:285-322, :658-659); RESET DEFAULTS stages the shipped values | Settings stored by Meitou instead of pg_config.json ui_settings (task 2.6, decision D6, `src/Ui/PGSettingsUI.cs`, `Core/PGConfig.SettingsPage.cs`): Meitou keeps the page in `meitou.user.json` (`modSettings`, global per install; a value equal to the default is not kept, ModSettingsRows.cs:131-140). At start, once per process: Load reads pg_config.json as before (its own `ui_settings` block included), the page is registered with those FILE values as its defaults, and only the values the player changed (differing from the file's) are applied over the file as a `ui_settings` block through Core's own reader, so each is range-checked, bookie bounds are put on whole tens, "minimum exceeds maximum" keeps the file's range with the same log lines. The page never writes pg_config.json; SaveEditableSettings is not called. Meitou's Reset / Restore defaults put back the FILE's values, not the shipped ones (the original's RESET DEFAULTS staged the shipped ones). The Load summary line is logged a second time ("... with the settings page values applied ...") when the page changed something. A pg_config.json that exists but cannot be read, or that Parse keeps nothing from (invalid JSON, a root that is not an object, a wrong version), takes no page values, as the original then read no ui_settings (PGConfig.cpp:841-847, 356-379): only the shipped values apply, the page shows them (logged "Proving Grounds: pg_config.json was not read, so the settings page values are not applied either; the shipped values stay in force"), and the player's kept values stay in meitou.user.json for a start whose file reads again. A MISSING file (the shipped default written) still takes the page values, where the original had none (its ui_settings lived in that file). After a change the page shows the new value at once; the original reloaded the values in force when the page opened (GetEditableSettings, PGSettingsUI.cpp:780-787, PGConfig.cpp:976-987), so a restart value showed its old value until a restart. A value Meitou keeps although Changed refused it (Meitou keeps the value, then shows the throw as the notice, ModSettingsRows.cs:115-128), for example a minimum above its maximum, makes Core reject that whole section at the next start, including that section's other valid changes (recruitment or bookie: the file's range stays), as the original's Save refused the whole page | Meitou has its own settings store; writing ui_settings too would make two sources. Meitou cannot tell "never saved" from "saved equal to the default", so a page value that equals the file's follows the file: a player who later hand-edits pg_config.json sees that edit for every row they did not change on the page (in the original, the first SAVE SETTINGS pinned all 19 values in ui_settings) |
| Page values typed in an edit box (parsed with msvcr100 `strtod`: inf/nan rejected, `d` read as an exponent; whole numbers only for integer rows; decimals rounded to 0.01, bookie stakes to tens) or set with sliders whose steps were 5 / 10 / 100 / 0.1 / 0.01 (PGSettingsUI.cpp:116-133, 326-385, 397-417); the minimum's slider stopped at the current maximum and the other way round (GetLimits) | Meitou sliders only (no text box, so no strtod), clamped to the original's full range and snapped to a step that reaches every value the original's text box accepted: 1 for whole numbers, 10 for bookie stakes, 0.01 for decimals; the arrow keys move one step. Ranges too wide for a usable slider keep the original's range; their hint says so (" Port note: the slider spans the whole range; the arrow keys move it one step."). Minimum and maximum are independent sliders: a minimum above the maximum is shown and kept, the page shows the original Save button's refusal ("Recruitment settings are outside their supported ranges." / "Bookie limits must be ordered multiples of 10 Cats.", PGConfig.cpp:991-1024) as its notice, and at start Core rejects that section as for a ui_settings block (file range kept, logged) | Meitou settings are bool, number sliders or lists (CAP 8). Numbers display as Meitou prints them (`0` / `0.##`), not `%d` / `%.2f` |
| "F8 debug menu" shows Enabled / Disabled, cycled with - / + buttons | A list: debug menu key Off / O / N / V (Off = disabled; a file with `f8_opens_debug_menu: true` reads as O, the first key). The key row keeps the original's hint ("When enabled, F8 toggles the debug menu. When disabled, F8 does nothing.") with the label "Debug menu key". Crowd performance (a list with the original's captions High / Normal / Potato) and the key apply at once like a save did (PGConfig.cpp:1069-1070): the page's Changed publishes them and the system applies them in its next serial step (a page change made with no world loaded applies when one is) | F8 is Meitou's screenshot key (overview §1) |

The golden vectors are made with glibc, whose `strtoul`/`atoi` overflow differently, so they hold no 10+ digit run; the MSVC overflow cases are hand tests |
| pg_config.json parsed by rapidjson (`kParseCommentsFlag \| kParseTrailingCommasFlag`, PGConfig.cpp:351-352, 765-766, 1034-1035, 1055-1056) | `System.Text.Json` (`JsonDocument`, trailing commas, comments skipped) behind a rapidjson-shaped view in `Core/PGConfig.cs` (`RjDocument`, `Rj`): first-match `FindMember`, rapidjson's int/double typing by literal, NUL-terminated `GetString()`, the text cut at the first NUL byte, NaN/Infinity rejected, a number that does not fit a double a parse error, BOM skipped once | rapidjson is not in the source release (it cannot be compiled or copied); every validation rule and message of PGConfig.cpp is kept and hand-tested (`Tests/Core/PGConfigTests.cs`). Residual edges, all on files a user would have to break on purpose: (1) the parse-error line `... is not valid JSON at offset %u (%s) ...` gives System.Text.Json's offset and reason, not rapidjson's (identical only for an empty document: "The document is empty."); (2) invalid UTF-8 inside the file is a parse error (rapidjson, without `kParseValidateEncodingFlag`, accepts it); (3) rapidjson's "number too big" boundary is its exponent count (`0e400` is an error there), the port's is the value overflowing a double; (4) rapidjson's default (not full-precision) double parsing can differ from the correctly rounded value in the last bit for long fractional literals; (5) an unpaired high-surrogate escape (`\uD800`) and a number that overflows a double are parse errors in both; where System.Text.Json only finds them when the value is decoded, the port reports offset 0; (6) a lone LOW-surrogate escape (`\uDC00`) is rejected by the port, while rapidjson 1.1 accepts it (it encodes it as three invalid UTF-8 bytes); (7) leftover catalogue keys (`ReportLeftovers`, PGConfig.cpp:622-631) are reported in UTF-16 ordinal order of the C# strings, where `std::map<std::string>` orders the UTF-8 bytes: identical for ASCII keys, and a non-ASCII key could be reported at a different place in the list; comments are blanked (with spaces, offsets kept) in a copy before parsing, following rapidjson's rules (`//` to the next `\n` only, so a CR-only file fails as in the original; an unterminated `/* */` is an error), because System.Text.Json rejects a comment between a member name and its `:` that rapidjson and the overlay scanner (PGConfig.cpp:770) accept |

| pg_config.json file access: `_wfopen_s`, `GetFileAttributesW`, `MoveFileExW`, `DeleteFileW` inside PGConfig.cpp | `IConfigFiles` (Read with Missing / Read / Unreadable, Write, MoveReplace, Delete) implemented by the glue; Core sequences the calls exactly as the C++ (default file written in place, settings saved through `pg_config.json.tmp`) and keeps the 1 MiB guard | Core stays file-free; same files, same order |
| `ResultsLayout::PortraitInitialUtf8(const char*)` (ResultsLayout.h:11-39) takes the fighter name as the engine's narrow, NUL-terminated byte string, which can hold any bytes | `ResultsLayout.PortraitInitialUtf8` has the same byte logic over a `ReadOnlySpan<byte>` (a 0 byte or the end of the span is the terminator; a cut sequence gives "?", never an exception) and returns a C# `string` decoded from the accepted bytes, plus a `string?` overload for the glue (null gives "?", otherwise the name is encoded as UTF-8 and the byte version runs) | the byte version is exact (4,000+ golden vectors with every lead byte, overlong, surrogate and upper-bound edge, `Tests/Golden/LayoutsGoldenTests.cs`), and every sequence it accepts is well-formed UTF-8, so the string is lossless. A C# string cannot hold malformed UTF-8, so the original's "?" for malformed input only arises through the byte overload. Note: a lone UTF-16 surrogate in a .NET string (`"\uD800"`) is encoded by .NET as EF BF BD (U+FFFD), which the byte rules accept, so the string overload returns the replacement character "\uFFFD" where the byte string would never have arisen in the C++ (names are UTF-8 there and never hold a lone surrogate); only a name the glue built from invalid UTF-16 could show it |
| `TownChallengePolicy::Unhex` (TownChallengePolicy.h:240-257) turns hex into any bytes, stored in a `std::string`; the compact codec (`DecodeLegacy`, `Decode`, `ReadHistory`) accepts a card whose names hold bytes that are not valid UTF-8 | `Core/TownChallengePolicy.Codec.cs` `Unhex` decodes the bytes strictly as UTF-8 and returns false on invalid UTF-8, so such a text does not decode (the card is left untouched, as on any other rejection) | a C# string cannot hold invalid UTF-8. The port never writes such text (Hex writes the UTF-8 bytes of C# strings, identical to the C++ for ASCII and valid UTF-8 names), and the codec has no caller in the shipped 1.3.4 plugin (the save stores the card as JSON, ArenaSnapshotJson.cpp:457-460); only a hand-edited text can hit it. `Tests/Golden/challenge.json` marks the C++ cases that produced invalid UTF-8 (`inv`); the golden fuzz writes the numbers 4294967295/9999 as `04294967295`/`09999` so they never decode as invalid text by accident. `Decode`'s `value.size() > 100000` counts the UTF-8 bytes of the C# string, as the C++ counts the std::string's |
| The compact codec reads with `std::istringstream` `operator>>` (TownChallengePolicy.h:274-317, 483-499, 520-572); the shipped build uses MSVC's library | `TownChallengePolicy.CStream` reproduces libstdc++ (the golden vectors are made with g++): C whitespace skipped; int = optional sign + decimal digits, fails outside the int range; unsigned fails above 4294967295 and negates a `-` value modulo 2^32 (`-1` reads as 4294967295); bool reads a long, only 0/1 (`01` and `-0` are accepted); double collects `[sign]digits[.digits][e[sign]digits]` (`e` only after a digit) and fails on overflow; a number stops at the first other character (`1e3` read into an int is 1, then `e3` is the next word) | the codec has no caller in 1.3.4, so not verified against MSVC. Input classes where MSVC might differ and that only a hand-edited text can contain: a `-` sign read into an unsigned (MSVC's num_get goes through strtoul, believed to wrap the same), a leading `+`, leading zeros, a double that underflows to 0 or a subnormal (the port accepts it, as libstdc++ does), `1e3`-style tokens in an int field. Every text `Encode` writes uses only well-formed in-range numbers and single spaces, where both libraries agree |
| `TownArenaRuntimePolicy::DiscoveryIndex::byIdentity` is a `std::map<std::string, Actor*>` (ordered by the key bytes) | `SortedDictionary<string, TActor?>` with `StringComparer.Ordinal` (UTF-16 code units) | identical order for ASCII keys; two non-ASCII keys (an identity built from a non-ASCII name) could iterate in a different order. The glue swaps the map into the registry snapshot and iterates it (TownMatchmakingRuntime.cpp:89-90, TownArena.cpp:185-186 and :731): the town glue (M7) must keep this in mind, and should key by UTF-8 bytes if the order turns out to matter |

The notes of task 1.6 (PGConfig row above) assumed 1.1 | `Core/RapidJson.cs` translates rapidjson v1.1.0 (commit f54b0e47, `golden/fetch_rapidjson.sh`), and `Tests/Golden/snapshot.json` is made by compiling the REAL ArenaSnapshotJson.cpp and ArenaSnapshotRepository.cpp against that version: the reader (error texts and byte offsets), DOM number typing, the PrettyWriter bytes (Grisu2 doubles) and full-precision strtod, including 1.1.0's own answers where they are not correctly rounded (`2.4703282292062327e-324` reads as 5e-324, IEEE gives 0; `10e308` passes 1.1.0's exponent check and reads as the garbage -3.09e-308, IEEE gives +inf; the codec rejects it either way) | if the shipped DLL used another rapidjson release, number edges like these could read differently; PGConfig.Json.cs (task 1.6, System.Text.Json) is unchanged and not shared with this codec, because the snapshot's messages need rapidjson's own parse errors, offsets and SAX order, which System.Text.Json does not give |
| Three snapshot inputs are undefined behaviour in rapidjson 1.1.0's strtod: a zero significand that misses the fast path (`0e300`, `0.0e-30`: `__builtin_clzll(0)` / `_BitScanReverse64` of 0), a value below ~1e-330 written with many digits (`1.234567890123456789e-342`: `GetCachedPower10` reads outside its 87-entry table; the g++ build crashes), and a BigInteger shift by a whole number of 64-bit digits (`operator<<=` memmoves from index `count_-1`, reading uninitialized digits) | The port gives the correctly rounded value 0.0 for the first two (a nonzero tiny literal is then "nonzero number is too small to represent as a double") and reads zeros for the third | only a hand-edited sidecar can hold such a number; the original may crash or read garbage there. The golden contains none of them: the generator runs clean under `-fsanitize=address,undefined` and gives identical output with `-ftrivial-auto-var-init=zero` and `=pattern` |
| RewardTransaction.h: `Accounting` / `Binding` / `Inventory` / `Action` are C++ structs with virtuals, and the glue derives from BOTH `Binding` and `ReservedAccounting`, passing `static_cast<Binding&>(*this)` to the base constructor (RewardDelivery.cpp:439-446, PrisonerRecruitment.cpp:34-38); `ArenaLedger::Purchase(int amount = 0)` is a non-explicit constructor | `Accounting` an abstract class (default `Accept` kept), `IBinding` / `IInventory` / `IAction` interfaces; `ReservedAccounting` keeps the C++ constructor (`ArgumentNullException` on null), and the glue derives from the abstract `SelfBoundReservedAccounting : ReservedAccounting, IBinding` (abstract `Matches`; its constructor binds to `this`, the base parameterless constructor is `private protected`), so a glue class that forgets its binding does not compile; no implicit int-to-Purchase operator (no caller converts implicitly) | C# has no multiple class inheritance and cannot pass `this` to a base constructor; same method sets, call order and results, no effect on play. Evidence: `Tests/Golden/ledger.json` for the public constructor and the call order; the self-binding constructor only by the hand tests `Tests/Core/RewardTransactionTests.cs` (`SelfBound`, `A_glue_class_that_is_its_own_binding`) |
| Snapshot strings are the bytes of `std::string`; the codec reads and writes bytes | `ArenaPersistence.DecodeSnapshot(byte[])` / `EncodeSnapshot(..., ref byte[] json, ...)` take and give the same bytes; the snapshot's strings are C# strings (a decoded string has passed the reader's UTF-8 check, so it converts exactly). Sorting by id compares UTF-8 bytes, as `std::string::operator<` does. A lone UTF-16 surrogate in a C# string (which a `std::string` cannot hold) is written as the 3-byte form of its code unit, so the encoder's own re-check rejects it ("Invalid encoding in string.") | none for valid text; the original likewise refuses to write invalid bytes. `std::sort` (not stable) is a stable sort here: they differ only between rows with the same id, which the writer's re-check rejects as duplicates. With two rows sharing an id that also differ in a field the reader rejects (or one with a non-finite mmr), the order decides which error the encode reports (the duplicate id, the other field's message, or the writer's index): stable order here, introsort order in the C++ once the table is past the insertion-sort threshold (libstdc++, which made the goldens: 16 elements; MSVC, the original's build: 32) |
| PGLog (PGLog.cpp:9-88) writes the bytes of `std::string` lines with `_wfopen_s(.., L"ab")` / `fwrite` / `fclose` beside the DLL, stamps `GetSystemTime`, builds the session id from `GetSystemTimeAsFileTime` + `GetCurrentProcessId` during static init, and warns once with `OutputDebugStringA` | `Core/PGLog.cs` builds every line (same texts, envelope, `\u%04x` quoting, session id format `%08lx%08lx-%lu`, the process-wide id source `NextId` for match / market / challenge ids (the per-line `record` is CombatBalanceLog's own counter), warn-once); the glue's `IPgLogSink` appends the line as UTF-8 to `Directory + name`, gives the UTC time and receives the warning; the glue calls `PGLog.Install(sink, fileTimeHigh, fileTimeLow, processId)` at startup, and nothing is written (no line, no warning) before that, where the C++ sink existed from DLL load; `Install` refuses a null sink and a second call (the session id stays fixed). A sink member that throws counts as a failed write (warned once, never rethrown) and a throwing clock skips the line: the Win32 calls of the C++ cannot throw, so this only adds safety for a faulty glue sink. Quote escapes UTF-16 code units below 32 (the same set as the C++'s bytes below 32) and copies the rest | C# strings are UTF-16 and Core does no file IO; identical bytes for UTF-8 text. ### Settings page strings not reproduced (task 2.6)

The page is Meitou's (see "Settings stored by Meitou" above); these PGSettingsUI.cpp texts have no place in it:

- string: F8 debug menu — the row is "Debug menu key" (overview §1)
- string: Enabled — the key row shows Off / O / N / V
- string: Disabled — the key row shows Off / O / N / V
string: Unsaved setting. Save to keep this change. — Meitou keeps a change at once; no status line
string: Enter a whole number or decimal value; the previous value was restored. — no text box, so no typed value to reject
string: Value was clamped to the nearest supported limit. — Meitou clamps silently
- string: Restart required — the popup's message is in the hints; there is no popup
- string: OK — no popup
string: Saved. Crowd performance applies next bout; F8 applies immediately. Other gameplay settings require restart; shop-price JSON was left unchanged. — no Save button of ours; the hints say which rows need a restart
string: Shipped defaults are staged. Select Save Settings to write them; shop prices remain unchanged. — Meitou's Reset / Restore defaults
string: Proving Grounds Settings — the page is Meitou's Settings window
string: PROVING GROUNDS CONFIGURATION — no heading of ours on Meitou's page
string: Gameplay changes require a restart; crowd performance applies to the next bout after saving. Gear, weapons, supplies, licences, remain in pg_config.json. — no description area of ours on Meitou's page
string: Config changes are staged until saved. — no status line
string: SAVE SETTINGS — Meitou's own Save
string: RESET DEFAULTS — Meitou's own Reset / Restore defaults
- string: CLOSE — Meitou's own close button
- string: PG CONFIG — no button injected (Meitou lists the group under the mod)
- string: - — lists are Meitou's choice widgets
- string: + — lists are Meitou's choice widgets

## A target left after the disengage (task 3.5)

Meitou-only, no C++ counterpart. After a disengage (match end, `pg stop`, the save's abort) the C++'s endCombatMode leaves no attack target.
In Meitou an opponent that lost the initiative roll keeps a yield record (`YieldTo` / `YieldUntil`, 0.5 s from the end of its block or dodge,
CombatSystem.Act.cs:766-769) that `CombatSystem.Disengage` does not clear (it drops only the stopped character's own slot,
CombatSystem.cs:608-631), and while it lasts the combat step turns the stopped character on its yielder whether it fights or not
(`TurnOnYielder`, CombatSystem.Act.cs:531-545). With no fight task nothing clears that target again: a fighter "still targeting" its opponent
long after the match (seen once in task 3.4's fight.sh, reproduced in task 3.5's spar-stop: PG-WATCH target set, row null, not in combat;
the senses then tag the pair Enemy, AggroSystem.cs:1559-1590). The port adds `FightStarterLogic.SweepStaleTargets`: for 3 s (90 ticks)
after a full disengage, every serial step gives a fighter with a target but no fight (not in combat, no attack task, not down) one more
StopCommand for the next tick; a fighter that fights again (a new order, a new match) is never touched. Side effects to know: the sweep's
StopCommand also ends whatever else that fighter does then (its player half clears the order queue): a move order given to such a fighter
within the 90 ticks is cancelled; and a fighter benched in a following Teams 1v1 match within the window is not taken off the sweep (only
EngageMatch does that), so it is stopped too if it carries such a stray target (task 4.3's walk-in must not be cut by it).

Null for a building with neither a functionality nor an inventory (MachineData.cs:323-327: the Arenas 183 / 334), whose record is then its only match | Same behaviour for every shipped record (47 is the function of Registry 45 and Registry Town 195, 60 of Banner 42, 57 of Leaderboards 53, 273 of Leaderboards Town 272). Would differ only for a placed building whose use node carries another functionality than its record's first, which Meitou has no way to express |

| A button acts on the MyGUI event its delegate is bound to: `eventMouseButtonClick` (most buttons) on the LEFT RELEASE over the widget it was pressed on, and inside MyGUI's double-click time (0.25 s since the last click, a timer that expires when the widget under the pointer changes) on any left release; nothing when the pressed widget was hidden or disabled by then; `eventMouseButtonPressed` (the Arena presets) on the press (MyGUI 3.2.2 MyGUI_InputManager.cpp injectMousePress :218-281, injectMouseRelease :283-333; MyGUI_Widget.cpp :986-987) | Task 4.4b: each window declares its buttons' C++ event (`PgScreen.ButtonEvent`; tables `ArenaUINames` / `ResultsUINames` / `LeaderboardUINames.ButtonEvent`, tested). Meitou's `Activated` for a Click button's left press is held (`Core/Glue/UiButtonClick.cs`, MyGUI's rules above, tested) and sent as `UiEventKind.Click` at the left release the `PlayerInterface.MouseUp` postfix hit-tests (after the screen's release events, as MyGUI raises Released before Click); a Press button is sent on the press (`UiEventKind.Press`); the Arena rows keep their own pointer (`ArenaRowPointer`). Meitou's router does nothing else with our screens' events (ScreenRouter.FaithfulAction has no branch for them). Meitou's keyboard activation of a button (Enter on a focused button or a window's default button, MyGuiScreenHost.Submit / ActivateFocused) raises `Activated` outside a mouse press: it acts at once, as before. A left button the system says is up without a release seen (PointerButtons), or a new left press, forgets the held press (its release was skipped). The widget under the pointer is followed for the timer by a `PlayerInterface.MouseMove` postfix and at each arrange; a widget disabled or hidden since the press loses MyGUI's focus either way (setEnabled and setVisible both unlink it), so the two are one case | Meitou's faithful screens activate every button on the press (MyGuiScreenHost.cs:504-508); the releases exist since 4.5b. MyGUI Buttons take no Enter key, so the keyboard activation is Meitou's own (kept). The double-click timer counts real time (MyGUI's frame time) and is kept apart from the Arena rows' (both expire on any change of widget, so they agree) |
| A click acts at once (the UI and the game share one thread) | A click is a `UiClick` command applied in the next tick's serial Inputs; mod commands wait while the game is paused (CAP 7.0, World.Interface.cs:22-40), so a click made in a paused game applies when it resumes | Meitou's window/simulation split. Our windows are non-modal, so opening one does not pause the game (a modal one would, PlayerInterface.Ui.cs:1006) |
| Code opens / hides its window directly (`ShowWindow`, `setVisible`) | The sim half asks through its published model (a request serial, open or close); the window side applies it at the next interface arrange with the HUD shown (a hidden overlay contribution, CAP 7.1) or at the open window's bind; under the title, the character editor or a conversation the request waits. A window closed from the window side (Escape, its close button, CloseAll) is reported to the sim half as a `UiClick` of kind `Closed`, which then stops saying open; a UI reload (`LoadUi` again) registers the windows again and re-applies the latest request, so an open window opens again | No window access from the simulation thread |
| A new world hides the windows (`AbandonWorldState`, ArenaUI.h:13) | The window side closes our open windows when the published system (the world) changes | Same effect, from the window thread |
| `PortraitManager::setImageWidget(character, image)` gives any character's portrait (ResultsUI.cpp:96-108) | The open windows' characters get cells of the renderer's portrait atlas after the squad bar's and the character trading window's, before the conversation's two (a postfix on `TradeWindows.CharacterTradePortraits()`); the ImageBox shows `meitou:portraits` with the game's own `PortraitBar.PortraitRect(cell)`; with no free cell (a squad of 30, or 28 while a conversation shows) the picture is hidden and the name alone shown | No portrait API in Meitou (CAP 7.4; spike S7 VERIFIED live) |

string: Presets: left-click to load, right-click to save. — kept verbatim: task 4.5b gives the Arena window a real right-click (row above), so neither this hint nor "Double-click or drag to assign. Right-click to remove." changes (the 4.4 plan's stand-in wording is gone).

## ArenaIngress (task 4.3b)

`Core/Glue/ArenaIngressLogic.cs` (every ArenaIngress.cpp function in order; the Registry / Leaderboard approach since task 4.2, its rows in "ContextMenuHooks and the approach"), host
`src/Game/PgIngressHost.cs`, surface `src/Game/ArenaIngress.cs`. Formation points from `Core/ArenaFormation.cs` (task 4.3a).

| Original | In this port | Why / effect |
|---|---|---|
| `ParkCharacterInPlace` (ArenaIngress.cpp:599-616): removeJob(MOVE_CUS_ORDERED), clearAllAIGoals, PASSIVE + HOLD on, AGG off, a `STAND_STILL` goal for a match prisoner or town fighter, reThinkCurrentAIAction | A player character: its walk ended (`PgEngine.EndWalk`, a GoTo task through `TaskStart.End`) and Passive + Hold on where it stands (`SetBenchStance(id, true)`; its stance before the staging comes back through FightStarter's restore, recorded at ingress start). An NPC: its walk ended and `CharacterCold.Engaged = true` (`HoldStill` the first time, `ReassertHold` on every later park, i.e. every countdown tick) | CAP 5: the AI never holds a player character, and Meitou has no STAND_STILL goal; Engaged is the engine's "the AI leaves it alone" flag (spike S10). AGG has no counterpart (FightStarter's note) |
| The C++'s `STAND_STILL` goal ends when the next order or goal replaces it | The NPCs the staging holds with Engaged are let go (`ReleaseHold`, which also asks the AI to replan) where the staging ends: `ClearPendingState` (a cancel, a lost fighter or site, and just before `StartMatch`) and `AbandonWorldState` | Port only: nothing else would clear Engaged. Between the release and the match's attack order (next tick) the AI may start a routine walk; the attack order replaces it. No NPC fights before M6/M7, so this is not reached in M4 |
| `IssueMove` (:428-453): for a town fighter `clearAllAIGoals()` and RUN speed | Not done: `PgIngressHost.IssueMove` sets the move stance (Hold, Passive off) and gives one `MoveOrder` per character | No town fighter exists before M7; task 7.3 adds the AI clear and the run speed |
| A fighter given a staging move order walks to its mark (Kenshi steers it out of whatever it stands on) | Known Meitou 0.17.3 limit, not ours (task 4.8): a squad member left idle near the Leaderboard takes its seat (job SitAround, task Operate); a move order from there toward the Arena (south of the board in arena-full's layout) gives it a path (moveFlags HasPath) but it never moves (speed 0). The game's own move order does the same (agent `order move`, Jobs on, no mod code involved), while a move order east leaves the seat at once. The player sees the fighter stay seated at the board while the window says "Waiting for N fighters to reach formation..." (player characters have no staging time limit, as the C++); a manual move order off the seat in another direction frees the character (seat-probe step 1; not checked live: that the waiting staging then completes, which the next re-issue should do) | Evidence: `tools/scenarios/seat-probe.sh` (runs seat-probe-3/4/5, 2026-11-14). arena-full builds the Leaderboard 1,150 from the Registry so its walkers do not sit there |
| The fighters walk to their formation marks and the countdown starts once every one stands on its mark (no staging time limit for player characters) | Unverified live, cause not measured (task 4.8): in arena-full the walk from the Formation line to the countdown took avb ticks 3635 -> 4242 (~20 s), 1v1 9510 -> 18451 (~298 s) and ffa 22014 -> 31978 (~332 s); in both slow bouts the countdown started with c:2.0 at 29.95 (1v1) / 29.98 (ffa) from its mark, inside the 30-unit check (`live-arena-full.steps`, 2026-11-14). Candidates: a Meitou walker caught on the placed Arena's corner (as the scenario's comment says) or a mark inside the building's geometry, so the walker only comes within the 30 units after a long time | Commit 206c214 raised arena-full's countdown wait from 180 s to 480 s: the C++ has no staging limit for player characters (its 30 s / 20 s / 90 s limits are a town NPC's), so the longer wait is not a loosened check; the 30-unit check is unchanged. To measure: the slow walker's probe (moveFlags, goal, speed) through the wait |
| `Tick`'s first test: a busy town arena while `ou->isPaused()` keeps the clock and ticks the announcer's approach (:1418-1423) | Copied, but never true: a paused world runs no serial step (World.Interface.cs:16-40) and PgClock is frozen meanwhile, so the dt after a pause is one tick, as the C++'s refreshed `g_lastTick` gives | Same effect |
| `Begin` accepts a start while a save is being prepared | Port only: refused with SparSession's save-in-flight text (`Saving the game: start the match after the save`) while a save prepared by the save safety is not captured yet | Task 2.5's rule: nothing may restart arena activity between the save prepare (which cancels the ingress) and the capture. The C++ prepares and saves in one call |
| A Teams 1v1 walk-in fighter still watched by the post-disengage sweep (Meitou-only, task 3.5) could get a StopCommand that ends its walk | `BeginWalkIn` takes the fighter off the sweep (`FightStarter.LeaveStaleSweep`) before its move order | Port only (the sweep is port only) |
| `hand g_boundRegistry / g_boundSite` and `building->getOrientation()` | `ArenaBuildingRef`s re-resolved each tick through `ArenaIdentity.Resolve` (task 4.1's rule); the site's orientation is `Quat.FromYaw(ArenaBuilding.Yaw)` | Same turn for an upright building, settled by code: Meitou's yaw is `YawOf(q) = atan2(forward.X, forward.Z)` with `forward = q * UnitZ` (TownBuildings.cs:534-539; a level building's quaternion read as (x, y, z, w), LevelFile.cs:201-202), and `Quat.FromYaw(t)` is Ogre's `FromAngleAxis(t, UNIT_Y)`, so `FromYaw(YawOf(q))` reproduces q's rotation when q has no tilt (worked for Scratch's Arena 183, yaw 1.4546: the marker (-80, 0, 0) goes to (-9.28, 0, 79.46) both ways). Only a tilt is dropped (every PG building stands upright). Live (arena-ingress): the turned formation is inside the pit, walkable, reached and faced |
| `Begin` (:1247-1393) and `ClearPendingState` / `FinishIngressAndStart` (:784-822, :927-949) assume no engine call throws | Port only: Begin marks the staging pending (Formation) as soon as the fighters, teams and site are stored, and a throw in the rest (Jobs off, the moves, the ring site, ...) is cleaned up by `ClearPendingState` (the fighters disengaged: Jobs and stance back), status `Ingress failed`, one log line, false. `ClearPendingState` resets its fields first and runs each side effect (move-job removal, hold release, disengage, announcer release) on copies under its own try/catch (logged once per step per world). A throw from `StartMatch` / `GetStatus` counts as a refused start (status `Ingress failed`), so the fighters are disengaged | A throw must never leave fighters with Jobs off and no owner (IsPending false: the save safety would not see them) |
| `Begin` reads `fighters[i]` / `teams[i]` for `i < count` | Port only: a fighter or team list shorter than the count is refused with the C++'s own `Invalid fighter selection` | The C++ would read past its arrays |
| `Begin` turns the escorts' Jobs off (`RememberMatchFighters(escorts)`, :1322-1323) but `ClearPendingState` disengages only the fighters (:803) | Kept as the C++: an escort keeps its remembered state (Jobs off) after a cancel until FightStarter forgets it | Kept quirk, noted for M6 (prisoner handlers are the only escorts) |
| `Cancel` (:1395-1411) returns match prisoners to their cages (`BeginReturnToCages`) | The port-only catch of a `Begin` that threw (row above) runs `ClearPendingState` only, not Cancel's prisoner branch, even if the throw came after `BeginHandlerUnlocks` | Decided in task 6.3 as the C++ behaves: the C++ Begin cannot fail after `BeginHandlerUnlocks`, and every false Begin is followed by ArenaUI's own `ReturnAllToCages` when the match includes a prisoner (ArenaUI.cpp:2434-2441, ported in ArenaUILogic's start), which forces every prisoner back, a released one included. So a throw there returns the prisoners through the caller, as any failed start does |
| `ou->getObjectsWithinSphere` site search for the walk-in (`BeginWalkIn` searches again near the fighter, :1729) | The same: `ArenaIdentity.FindSite` from the fighter's position, the C++ "site search" line each time | Copied |

## ArenaMedical (task 4.7)

`Core/Glue/ArenaMedicalLogic.cs` (SetProtocol, the block over the match view, ActionableAidNeed, NeedsStabilization, IssueStabilizeOrder, the
test verb), glue `src/Game/ArenaMedical.cs`; the policy is `Core/ArenaMedicalPolicy.cs` (M1).

| Original | In this port | Why / effect |
|---|---|---|
| Three hooks, `MedicalSystem::applyFirstAid`, `applyDoctoring` (robot repair) and `applyRigging` (splints) (ArenaMedical.cpp:167-209, 269-313): blocked = the original not called, `false` (not complete) returned, so the medic stays on its task and does nothing until allowed | One prefix on public `FirstAidSystem.Apply(World, in Effect)` (FirstAidSystem.cs:524), the serial commit of one treatment tick: wound closing, bandaging with a first aid or robot repair kit, kitless prisoner care and splints all go through it. A blocked tick is skipped (nothing bandaged, no kit drained, no XP), the treatment task stays, and it treats once allowed (the AvB quirk: at the match's end) | Meitou has one treat task and one apply step for all three; the same effect as the three C++ hooks. Install texts: the three C++ failure lines and Plugin.cpp's `ArenaMedical hooks install failed` all print when the one patch fails |
| The plan text said: refuse the task START (`ITaskHandler.Start`, FirstAidSystem.cs:271, or `Begin` :300) when the patient is blocked | Not done: the per-tick apply is blocked, as the C++ | The C++ never refuses the order or the start; refusing the start would drop the player's order instead of keeping the medic on it until the patient is eliminated or the match ends |
| Robot repair has its own order (`FIRST_AID_ROBOT`) and apply hook (`applyDoctoring`) | No separate repair task in Meitou: the treat task picks the robot kit for robotic parts (FirstAidSystem.cs:569-583); `FirstAidRobot` (row 60) is kept as the order's row by `IssueStabilizeOrder` | Covered by the same prefix |
| `IssueStabilizeOrder`: `medic->addOrder(NULL, FIRST_AID_ORDER \| FIRST_AID_ROBOT, target, false, true, pos)` | `TaskStart.Start(medic, Treat, row 25 \| 60, TaskSource.Goal) { Target, Resume = ResumeOf(Treat, Idle) }` (CAP 12, `PgEngine.StartTreat`); the order queue is not used | Meitou's code route for a treatment (CAP 12); no live use before M6 |
| The town-bystander half of the block (`BlockTownBystander`, the `shouldIHelpThisGuy` / `pickupObject` hooks), `TownAftercare::RecordTreatment` and the Scratch medic restock in the apply hooks | Not built (TO-DO 8.4 in ArenaMedical.cs) | Town bouts only (TownArena::OwnsMatch never holds in the player arena): M8 task 8.4 |

The protocol (C++ `g_protocol`, a process global, never saved, never reset by a load) is one process-wide value in the port too, not part of
the per-world system: same behaviour across saves and loads (Ringside Aid again at the next process start; a new game or a load in the same process keeps the
last value, as the C++). Callers that change it put it back themselves: task 4.5's Start pushes the window's value at every Start
(ArenaUI.cpp:2392), and M7 restores its saved protocol at a town bout's end and when the world unbinds (TownArena.cpp:414, :1814).

Kept C++ behaviour for M8 aftercare: under Bloodsport (and under Ringside Aid for a participant not yet eliminated, e.g. any AvB fighter) a
medic already on a participant keeps its treat task, skipping every tick, until the match ends (the C++'s blocked hooks return "not
complete" and never end the order). If that participant dies meanwhile, Meitou itself ends the treat task (a dead patient,
FirstAidSystem.cs:395-398); the mod does not keep or end it. The block only skips the treatment ticks; it never ends or refuses the medic's
task, so M8's aftercare finds the medic still on its patient after a Bloodsport bout. The block fails open (treatment runs) when the combat hooks' serial step has failed in that world, so a stale
match view cannot keep blocking (port only).

## Arena window (task 4.5a, rules half)

published view), host `src/Game/PgArenaUIHost.cs`, sim half `src/Game/ArenaUISim.cs`; the window half (layout, binding, clicks) is task 4.5b.

|---|---|---|

A removed character keeps its row until the same prune points; its reason is `Invalid` (as the C++'s `!isValid()` test) and its name the one it was last seen with | The C++ would read freed memory there. Same rows and texts otherwise |
| A row click names a row by its index (`"kind:index"` user string, :646, :757-785) in rows rebuilt at once | The window side sends the character the row showed (`ArenaRowClick(list, id)`, ...); the sim ignores it when no row of that list shows that character any more | The click lands a tick later (task 4.4): the roster may have changed in between; an index could then name another fighter |
| `ArenaIngress::HasLocationSite` (its site search writes the "site search" debug line) is called up to three times per 0.20 s refresh while the window is open (:1835, :1898, :1940) | One answer kept for 1 s per (location, bound Registry) (`SiteCheckCache`); asked again when either changes, when the window opens and at Start | Same answers at those moments; about 1 "site search" line per second instead of up to 15 |
| The 0.20 s refresh runs in ForgottenGUI's update every frame, timed by `GetTickCount` (:3172-3185), also while the game is paused | It runs in the system's frame-loop step (after PrisonerRecruitmentUI's, the C++ slot), timed by the sim clock: no refresh while the game is paused; commands made while paused apply when it resumes (task 4.4) | Meitou's window / simulation split (CAP 7.0). The status line and labels freeze during a pause |
| A click can only reach a handler through a visible window | A window command that reaches the sim half while the window is closed (sent just before Start or Recruit hid it, a tick later) is ignored, except the close itself | The click lands a tick after the window side sent it (task 4.4); the C++ could never run a handler of a hidden window |
| BalanceTeams sorts with `std::sort` (:2276), whose order of two candidates with the same score and the same name is unspecified | A stable sort: such candidates keep their roster order | Deterministic; differs only for two fighters with equal score and equal name |
| The fighter card's `strengthActual()` / `dexterityActual()` (:567-568) are the current, condition-dependent values | Since task 7.2: Meitou's own value for those stats, `stat x scale x MedicalState.StatMultiplier(stat)` (internal `CombatSystem.Eff` with no gear term for these two stats, computed from its public pieces in `src/Game/ArenaCombatProfile.cs` `Actuals`); a character without a medical state shows its stored levels. `toughness()` = stored toughness x Scale, as Meitou's own reader. (Before 7.2 the card showed the stored levels.) | Meitou has no public getter of these names; the formula is its documented port of the original's stat getter (CombatSystem.Act.cs:40-48) |
| The C++ widgets keep whatever a function last wrote (e.g. the location buttons' highlight is written only by UpdateLocationHighlight, so binding a Banner leaves the Arena button highlighted until a mode or location click; AbandonWorldState closes the balance menu without UpdateBalanceMenuVisibility, so a reopened window still shows "Back" with By Skill / By Rating) | Each written value is kept in a field at the same moment and published; quirks like these are kept | Copy |

The held buttons come from the MouseDown / MouseUp postfixes and are reconciled each arrange with the system's buttons (`GetAsyncKeyState`, as the C++ polls VK_LBUTTON, ArenaUI.cpp:1205; Core/Glue/PointerButtons.cs): Meitou skips MouseUp under a loading screen, during a cinema or settings-panel drag, while its settings panel shows and on a lost focus, and its InputState never records a press the interface took; a press is forgotten only when the system still says up two loop passes after it first said so (passes counted by a prefix on `GameLoop.Frame`; mouse events reach the interface only in the DoEvents near the start of a pass, GameLoop.cs:1235, and the first sighting may come from inside that DoEvents, so a whole later DoEvents must have run; a paused game with a still pointer may run passes more slowly, so the forget comes later, which is safe; without the `GameLoop.Frame` member nothing is reconciled, one log line); a press made with no physical button down (the agent channel's click, `pg window drag`, a fast tap already up at dispatch) lives until its own release; Windows only (the Vulkan GameLoop; macOS's MetalGameLoop is not counted); elsewhere nothing is reconciled and only a new interface or world clears | Meitou's faithful screens raise no widget release, click-on-release, double-click or drag event (MyGuiScreenHost.cs:451-512): only the event source differs. Reference pictures, compared with the live run of `tools/scenarios/results-board.sh` at 1920 x 1080 (screenshots
`_shots/provinggrounds/results-board-results.png`, `results-board-leaderboard.png`): the structure matches (title rails, centred header and
mode line, bordered cards bottom-aligned with the champion's taller portrait, rank colours gold / silver; the board's "Champions of the Pit"
podium, rank captions, details line, centred Close). What differs, all from the renderer or the font: the game palette (parchment, dark ink)
where the pictures use Dark UI (dark grey, light text); larger text, cards and portraits (font 26-27 against about 18); card text wraps at other
points; the wheel step. ws-11.jpeg also shows "MVP" on all three cards of a Teams 1v1 result, where ResultsUI.cpp:460-470 (PlaceCaption) gives
"MVP", "MVP #2", "MVP #3": the port follows the source (the picture is from another build). The board's subtitle reads "Player arena standings - "
in a new game that was never saved, as the C++ (a new game's save key is empty, LeaderboardStore.cpp:194-200; the key is written at the save, ArenaNativePersistence.cpp:224). One translation mistake was
found and fixed: captions lost their line breaks (an XML attribute's raw newline is read as a space; LayoutBuilder now writes `&#10;`).
