## ContextMenuHooks and the approach (task 4.2)

`Core/Glue/ContextMenuHookLogic.cs` (which right press is ours, the use rows, the "use" pointer and its reset), glue
`src/Game/ContextMenuHooks.cs` (the right press on the window thread, the order hook on the tick thread), `src/Game/SquadUtil.cs`; the approach
itself in `Core/Glue/ArenaIngressLogic.cs` (BeginApproachForUI, NotifyExternalOrder, the PendingOpenUI step of Tick and its helpers).

| Original | In this port | Why / effect |
|---|---|---|
| Kenshi's right-click menu (`ContextMenu::showContextMenu`, ContextMenuHooks.cpp:133-158) and the building's default task (`getDefaultTask` -> USE_TRAINING_DUMMY, :158-162) | Spike S3: a prefix on `PlayerInterface.PickedRightDown` (PlayerInterface.cs:870) reads `Build.PickSite(p)`; a finished Registry / Banner / Leaderboard of the player's skips the game's press (no SitePress: no move order, no Operate, no menu) and sends `ArenaInteract` (the site ref and the selection); the sim starts the approach on the next tick | Meitou has neither a default task nor a menu hook for a building; its right press decides both the quick order and the held menu (BuildMode.SitePress / SiteRelease, BuildMode.cs:1248-1306). Same result: the walk-up and the window, no vanilla order |
| An unfinished one of ours: its quick "use" is swallowed (`addOrder_hook` :243-247, "interact use ignored (not finished)") and a held right press still opens Kenshi's context menu (:150-157: identity line, "interact RMB ignored (not finished)", NotifyExternalOrder(NULL), then `showContextMenu_orig`) | Copied: the press is the game's, so its held menu (Build / Cancel Construction / Dismantle, BuildMode.UpdateSite :1358-1366) opens as without the mod; only the quick order is dropped, by a prefix on `BuildMode.SiteRelease` (:1266; the `PlayerOrders.Give` hook cannot do it: Meitou's quick order here is Build / the Engineer job, not a use row). The menu watch sends the identity line, the "ignored" line and the cancel when that menu opens | Kenshi's quick order on our buildings was a use row (getDefaultTask_hook :158-162); Meitou's is Build: both are dropped. Live: the agent cannot hold the button, so only the dropped quick order is checked (arena-open (3)) |
| A character, item, container ... in front of the building takes its own click (Kenshi's pick) | The prefix acts only when `Picking.PickAt(p).Kind` is None or Site (the only kinds vanilla gives to SitePress, PlayerInterface.cs:885), never keyed on the kind otherwise (S3: Registry/Banner/Arena None, Leaderboard Site) | Same precedence as vanilla Meitou |
| `SquadUtil::CollectSelectedCharacters` falls back to `gui->selectedPlayerCharacter` when the selection is empty (SquadUtil.cpp:83-89) | No fallback: an empty selection collects nobody (and the press is the game's) | Meitou keeps no active character apart from the selection (PlayerState.cs:81-121) |
| With nobody selected, `BeginApproachForUI` still opens the window at once while a match, ingress or walk-in runs (:1182-1186 before the selection test :1197-1203) | Copied: the sim publishes `InteractBusy` (SparSession running, a staged fight or a walk-in pending) and a right press with nobody selected on a finished one of ours is swallowed and sent while it is set (Meitou's PickedRightDown runs with no selection: PlayerInterface.cs:863); otherwise with nobody selected the press is the game's | The flag is published every 0.20 s, so it lags both ways: a press with nobody selected in the first 0.20 s of a match, ingress or walk-in is still the game's (which does nothing with nobody selected), and a press up to 0.20 s after one ends is still swallowed and sent: the sim (BeginApproachForUI, nothing running any more) answers "No characters selected" and nothing opens, where the C++ would have left the press to Kenshi (which also does nothing with nobody selected) |
| `getPositionMarker(from)` (ArenaIngress.cpp:490): with two or more use nodes the nearest to the walker, else the building's position-marker part | `WorldObjectLayout.PositionMarker` (WorldObjectLayout.cs:429-435), Meitou's copy of that fewer-than-two-nodes branch (FUN_14054b850). Proving Grounds.mod has exactly one `usenode1024` instance on Registry 45, Leaderboard 53, Registry Town 195 and Leaderboard Town 272 (local (-3, 2, 12)), none on Banner 42, and no part flagged `is for position marker`: the marker is null for every PG building, so the walk-up point is RegistryApproachPoint's fallback (local (-3, 10, 3) pushed 30 outward, about 34 from the centre), as in Kenshi ("stuck at the building origin") | None: same branch, same data. Measured live: Registry target 34.3 from its centre, Banner the same |
| `showContextMenu_hook` logs `LogInteractIdentity(what, "RMB")` for every menu target (:135-136), ours or not | Logged only for a right press on one of our buildings (the ArenaInteract command; for an unfinished one when its menu opens) | The window half cannot read the world to name another target, and the sim never sees a press that is not ours. A debug line only |
| `g_issuingManagedMove` marks the approach's own synchronous `addOrder` so NotifyExternalOrder ignores it (:446-448) | Meitou applies a `MoveOrder` a tick after it is given (CAP 5, `world.Defer`): the order hook recognises the approach's own moves (`ArenaIngressLogic.ConsumeOwnApproachMove`: each move IssueApproachMoves gave in the last 2 s, the same walker and target within 1 unit on the ground, used up on arrival); a move whose approach ended in the tick between (finished, or cancelled by the very order that arrives with it) or was replaced by another (a right press on a second building before the first's move arrived: each approach start and each ClearPendingState bumps a generation, an older generation's move is a leftover) is dropped | Port only. Without the drop a move given in the cancel's tick could arrive after the player's order and walk the character back to the building (about one cancel in twelve, the 0.40 s re-issue). A player move ordered exactly onto a target in flight counts as the approach's |
| `Character::addOrder` hook (:228-258) | A prefix on `PlayerOrders.Give` (OrderQueue.cs:224), the entry of every player order: a use row (USE_TRAINING_DUMMY, OPERATE_MACHINERY, OPERATE_AUTOMATIC_MACHINERY, PRETEND_TO_OPERATE_MACHINERY = TaskType 97, 87, 152, 221) on one of our player sites starts the approach (finished) or is dropped (unfinished): Give answers `OrderCheck.Gone`; any other order to a walker cancels its approach | Same. Meitou's order UI reaches the use rows only through paths the S3 prefix already takes (the held menu), so this branch is the C++'s safety net |
| "Any other right-click menu cancels a pending approach" (`showContextMenu(on)` -> NotifyExternalOrder(NULL), :154-155) | The window half watches `ContextMenuWindow.OpenMenu` once per interface arrange; a menu that opened sends `ArenaExternalMenu` (NotifyExternalOrder(none)) | Meitou opens every menu (character, site, bed, seat, container, door, portrait) by setting OpenMenu (ContextMenuWindow.cs:710, BuildMode.cs:1366, FurnitureUse.cs); there is no single open method to hook. Off when the WindowsPressTaken reads are missing (one log line at install) |
| The pointer: `getMouseCursor` / `changeMouseCursor(target)` give USE_CURSOR over our buildings (:151-171); RefreshArenaCursor / Tick reset it after the click (:280-288, :327-345) | A postfix on `PointerView.PickWithSelection` (PointerView.cs:175): "use" over one of ours (finished or not) with a selection; over the building just clicked, the game's pointer until the pointer leaves it | Meitou picks its pointer only with a selection through that method (and caches it until the pointer, modifiers or view change, :151-157), so the reset shows at the next re-pick |
| The window decides on the live building (`IsInteractUiBuilding`, finished) | The window half reads the published `InteractRecords` (the player's site records ArenaIdentity reads as ours, 0.20 s publish) and the snapshot's `Complete`; the sim resolves the building again and the C++ predicates decide (a stale decision only changes which log line is written) | Window thread: no world read (M4 UI rules). A building placed less than 0.20 s ago answers as the game's |
| Town buildings (Registry Town 195, Leaderboards Town 272, level placements) | `Build.PickSite` lists only the player's construction. Task 7.6: the Town Registry (195, a level building) is picked with `BuildingSelection.PickBuilding` (BuildingSelection.cs:104, CAP 6 option B) in the same PickedRightDown prefix, only when nothing is in front (`Picked.None`: a plain building takes a right press only then, WorldPicking.cs:290-296) and it is not a ruin; it sends ArenaInteract with the town building (placement key, Town source) and the sim runs the same approach (BeginApproachForUI; ArenaUI::ShowFromRegistry opens the town window). No "use" pointer over it (the pointer postfix reads the player's site records only). The town Leaderboard (272, ArenaIdentity::IsLeaderboard) goes the same way and opens the town board (ArenaIngress.cpp:966-980, :997-1004; task 7.6 review). It is a machine: Meitou picks its operator seat in front of it (`Picked.Seat`; found live: the press gave the game's "Leaderboards Town is already in use"), so the building's own seat counts as nothing in front (`SeatSpot.Building` = the building's key; a bench or chair of another building in front keeps its own click, review 2); a town building Meitou keeps as a level-placement site (a picked Site with `LevelPlacement`, its Id the town key) is routed the same way. A Town Registry or Leaderboard under repair (a level-placement site not Complete) logs the C++'s "interact RMB ignored (not finished)" and the press stays the game's (C++ :127-140); an unfinished town building's 'ignored (not finished)' line is logged at the right press (ArenaInteractIgnored), not when the game's menu opens; any other town house under repair is the game's | The C++ hook sees every Kenshi building; Meitou has no pick kind for a plain level building (CAP 6) |
| A right press acts at once | `ArenaInteract` is a mod command: in a paused game it waits and the walk starts when the game resumes (CAP 7.0, World.Interface.cs:22-40) | Visible only while paused |
| The money trade with Null (`showTradeWindow_hook` :198-225, Tick's pending trade UI :290-325): a hook on `ForgottenGUI::showTradeWindow(first, second, TW_MONEY_TRADING)` | A prefix on the private `DialogueSystem.ConversationEffect` (DialogueSystem.Effects.cs:150, spike S4): for a `DialogueActions.Trade` effect it reads first = the target (the player side of the deferred `OpenTrade(target, me)`, :158) and second = the speaker, decides with `RewardsDialoguePolicy.ResolveTrade(..., moneyTrade: true)`, only when Meitou would open a trade window (the speaker has a squad, `mc is { SquadId: >= 0 }`, :158; otherwise the effect runs as without the mod, as the C++ hook in showTradeWindow never sees such a trade); for Null's it sets `Conversation.Ended`, queues the Rewards window and skips the original (no `OpenTrade` deferred). Every Meitou dialogue TRADE is a money trade, so the `type == TW_MONEY_TRADING` test is always true. "inDialogue" is the world's DialogueSystem still publishing a conversation view (`PublishConversation()`), read on the sim thread | Meitou opens the trade window only from that effect; there is no window-open call to hook. The trade window never appears for Null (the C++ never shows it either: its hook returns without calling the original). The window opens in the frame loop after the dialogue ended (the next tick at the latest) |
| The money trade with the bookie (`showTradeWindow_hook` :178-196, `TownBookie::Show`) | Not ported yet: Scratch Bookie's trade opens the game's trade window | TO-DO M8 (TownBookie, task 8.1) |

## Diagnostics and balance logs (task 8.7)

Task 8.7a: the controlled diagnostic run's glue `src/Town/TownDiagnosticRun.cs` (one member per entry point of TownDiagnosticRun.h, each
delegating to `Core/TownDiagnosticRun.cs` over the store's saved state; owned by TownArena, `src/Town/TownArena.Diagnostic.cs`), its callers
(TownArena's host, `src/Game/DebugMenuSim.cs`, `src/Engine/CombatLogHost.cs`) and the load path (`Core/Glue/ArenaWorldState.cs`, fake-tested in
`Tests/Glue/ArenaWorldStateTests.cs`).

| Original | In this port | Why / effect |
|---|---|---|
| ActivateLoad (ArenaNativePersistence.cpp:360-372) runs inside the load hook: the snapshot activated, the announced bout restored, `RecoverPendingAfterLoad` when no bout was announced (a pending assignment is cancelled, with its "diagnostic_assignment_cancelled" line), the "Loaded" line, then for a diagnostic error the "diagnostic_persistence_error" line and the report; the lines carry the loaded world's game hours and save key | The same steps in the same order when the loaded snapshot is applied at the world's first serial step, after the system bound the combat log host to the world. When a save applies the snapshot before that step (ArenaWorldState.TryWrite), the run's state change, the "Loaded" line and the report happen with the snapshot (so that save holds the cancelled assignment), and the two balance-log lines wait for the world's first serial step: written then, after the held bout's restore, in their order, once | Meitou applies loaded mod data at the first serial step (CAP 9.1), and a save's `IModSaveData.Write(Stream)` gets no world: before the first serial step there is no world for the line's game hours. The lines' text and fields are the C++'s; only the moment they are written moves, and only in that window |

Task 8.7b: the debug menu's drawn-model clicks (`src/Ui/DebugMenu.cs` Click, `src/Engine/PgCommand.cs` DebugMenuPress, `src/Game/DebugMenuSim.cs` ApplyPress, `Core/Glue/DebugMenuLogic.cs` DebugMenuDrawn and Press; fake-tested in `Tests/Glue/DebugMenuLogicTests.cs`).

| Original | In this port | Why / effect |
|---|---|---|
| Each button's eventMouseButtonClick handler (DebugMenu.cpp:387-509) runs in the frame the button was drawn: a row selects `g_characters[atoi(index)]` from the last Refresh, an action reads the current `g_selected`, a diagnostic action the current run state and `g_confirmFresh` | Port only: the window's click sends DebugMenuPress with what the model the window last drew showed for that widget (a row: the key of its character; a rating / Marks action: the key of the selected character; the town buttons: the drawn tab; a diagnostic action: its label and enabled state; Prev / Next: the page label and enabled state; a tab: nothing). The sim refuses the press when its own state for that widget differs: nothing changes, no status text (Apply answers why). The handlers, texts and the confirmation step are the C++'s. The test-only `pg debug press` presses against the current published model as if drawn now | Meitou applies a window's command in a later sim tick than the frame it was drawn in, so a list paged or collected again, another selection, or a label already replaced (a second Pause / Resume, "Target preset" or Fresh click before the redraw) could be misread. A second Fresh click before the CONFIRM label was drawn is refused, so town records are never reset without the confirmation having been shown. The view's Revision is not compared (the diagnostics page refreshes every second and would refuse good clicks) |

Task 8.7c: every CombatBalanceLog event checked against the C++ text (CombatBalanceLog.cpp, PGLog.cpp:75-85) and every caller's arguments
against what the C++ passes or reads (SparSession.cpp:389, :411, :667, :716, :722; TownArena.cpp:360, :1277-1290; TownDiagnosticRun.cpp:68,
:77-79, :117, :134; ArenaNativePersistence.cpp:370). The files are `pg_combat_debug.log` / `pg_challenge_debug.log` in the mod folder, which
holds ProvingGroundsMeitou.dll (`ModContext.Folder` = `ModInfo.Folder`, the folder Meitou loads the DLL from). Offline proof that every event
kind is one parsable JSON line on its channel: `Tests/Core/CombatBalanceLogTests.cs` Every_event_kind_is_one_parsable_JSON_line_on_its_channel.
`diagnostic_run_id` (TownDiagnosticRun.cpp:65, `GetTickCount()`) stays on `PgClock.NowMs`, because it seeds the diagnostic matchmaking (`r.Seed = RunId ^ NextSequence`) and so must follow the general clock row ("One per-frame UI hook", line 17); effect: run ids depend on the world's sim time, where the C++'s came from the system uptime.

| Original | In this port | Why / effect |
|---|---|---|
| `match_result`'s `wall_seconds` is `GetTickCount64()` at the end less at the start (CombatBalanceLog.cpp:167, :199): real milliseconds, counting while the game is paused and at real speed whatever the game speed | `PgLogGlue.WallMs64()` = `Environment.TickCount64`, the same counter (until task 8.7c the sim clock's `PgClock.NowMs64`, which stopped while paused and ran faster at a higher game speed; it is removed) | none: the same counter as the C++ (row kept to record the 8.7c fix); a log value, no timer depends on it, so the sim-clock rule of "One per-frame UI hook" does not apply |
| A `match_aborted` line's reason is "safe_save", "invalid_fighter", "superseded" or "world_teardown" (CombatBalanceLog.cpp:166, :288; SparSession.cpp:411, :722) | Also the port-only reason "start_failed": SparSessionLogic.StartMatch's undo after a throw once the combat log started (see the row "A throw part-way through SparSession's start or stop" above). Kept, not removed: without it the open match would be closed later by "superseded" (the next start) or "world_teardown", naming a reason that did not happen. "superseded" itself is unreachable in the C++ (SparSession refuses a start while a match runs, SparSession.cpp:587); in the port it is reached only after a throw in the result block before the result line (the log stays open) | a reader of the logs sees "start_failed" only after a port exception (the step-failure log names it too) |
| TownBookie.cpp calls OpenMarket (:308), MarketEvent "wager_accepted" (:110), "market_combat_started" (:361), "market_cancelled" (:371), CloseMarket (:381) and Abandon (:408) | Called since task 8.1a (`src/Town/TownBookie.cs` over `Core/Glue/TownBookieLogic.cs`, each call fake-tested in `Tests/Glue/TownBookieLogicTests.cs`) exactly as this list asks; no market value is a GetTickCount64 one. The list 8.1a followed: OpenMarket in BeginNpcBout after its `Settle(-1)` and the team capture: the fighters and teams as given, `count`, `scoreA` / `scoreB` = the ability passed in (`ability[0]`, `ability[1]`), `eligible` = the computed `market` flag, `scheduledGameHours` = `TownArena::GetPlannedNpcStartHours()` at that moment (planned ? next NPC hours : 0), the Town standings, each fighter by `ProvingGroundsSystem.CombatFighter(world, id, Town)`; Wager: `MarketEvent("wager_accepted", Core.TownBookie.WagerFields(side, stake, payout))` after the ticket is set; CombatStarted: `MarketEvent("market_combat_started")` only when `market` and not yet `fighting`; CancelNpcBout: `MarketEvent("market_cancelled", ",\"reason\":" + PGLog.Quote(reason))` BEFORE its `Settle(-1)`; Settle: when `market \|\| fighting \|\| ticket.pending`, `winner` forced to -1 when not `Eligible()`, `CloseMarket(winner, ticket.side, ticket.stake, ticket.payout, ticket.pending ? (winner < 0 ? ticket.stake : winner == ticket.side ? ticket.payout : 0) : 0, ticket.pending)` before the ticket is settled; AbandonWorldState: `CombatBalanceLog.Abandon` first, before anything is cleared (it is idempotent after SparSession's own Abandon). All over the world's `CombatLogHost` (`ProvingGroundsSystem.CombatLog`) | the market's events are written; a write that throws is caught and counted (TownBookie row) |

Task 9.2b (the cost per tick): the three log files keep the original's per-line open, write and close (PGLog.cpp:42-46 `_wfopen_s "ab"`, `fwrite`, `fclose` per line, "Flush each event for long unattended runs/crashes"; `src/Engine/PgLogSink.cs` Append), not a kept-open stream: a kept-open handle would not match it when the player deletes, renames or locks a file between lines (the original recreates the file or skips just that line and retries on the next), or when two game instances share the mod folder (the original's appends interleave at the end of the file). What a player pays during a bout: measured live in a second bout in one session (`tools/scenarios/perf.sh` (4b), 60 spectators seated), 279 pg_debug.log lines (nearly all aftercare: "aftercare bed care", "hospital return", "patient motion", "medic slot" ...), 5 pg_combat_debug.log lines, 0 pg_challenge_debug.log lines; the writes in the fight's result tick took 2.8 ms of that tick's 12.4 ms, other ticks 1.0-1.2 ms when they write. The original paid the same per-line cost natively, on its frame thread.

`Core/Glue/RewardDeliveryLogic.cs` (every public function and deciding helper, fake-tested in `Tests/Glue/RewardDeliveryLogicTests.cs`, every
failure branch of the two-phase transaction with the ledger checked back where it began), glue `src/Game/RewardDelivery.cs`, the engine calls

|---|---|---|

(:778-782, :1031-1035) | The same text when the reward-item adapter may not start a purchase (`Items.CanStart`: a Rewards member missing, or a fighter refresh that threw); asked ONCE per purchase, before anything is reserved or taken out, never between its moves; one "rewards off" log line per process | The engine's factory always exists in Meitou; the by-name members (CharacterAssembly.RefreshEquipment, Inventory.Add) are what can be missing |
| The Rewards window runs a purchase synchronously in the click, against the offer on screen (RewardsUI.cpp:489-561) | A `RewardPurchase` command applied in the next Inputs; a Claim carries the offer the window showed and is NOT run when ResolveOffer now differs (not ok, empty status: the window keeps its status line; a debug line); the result is published as `PgSnapshot.LastPurchase` with the sender's sequence number | The port refuses a command whose offer changed since it was shown: two clicks before the window saw the first result would otherwise buy an offer nobody was shown. The other kinds rely on the sequence (task 5.3's window sends no new purchase while its last one has no result) |
| A purchase command with an unknown kind or item cannot happen (the buttons name real rows) | Refused with the C++ text "The reward item record could not be found." | A bad command (never a button) must not reach the catalogue readers, which clamp to the first row |
| An exception inside a purchase is not caught (KenshiLib code that throws would end the game) | After the reservation every purchase recovers from an exception: the new item out and destroyed, the previous grade given back, the reservation cancelled (each step best effort), answered with the failure text of the phase it came from, or the C++ "accounting could not be restored" text when the refund fails | Without it the ledger stays reserved for the session (no purchase and no arena save) and a previous grade taken out is lost |
| An exception anywhere in a purchase (a read before the reservation included) is not caught | The command answers not ok with the C++ text "The purchase failed and accounting could not be restored. Check Arena Marks and equipment before saving." and a Meitou error line; `LastPurchase` is always published for its sequence | What the exception left behind is unknown, so the C++ text that tells the player to check Marks and equipment; the window always gets a result |
| A failed rollback is only the status text (and RewardDiag) | Also a Meitou error line with the record and level (unlock for a licence) | The player is told to check Marks and equipment; the error log says which reward |

| `DestroyUndelivered(item, reason)` passes a reason text to `ou->destroy` | No reason | Meitou's item removal takes none (Items.Destroy) |
| "Prisoners retain Marks but cannot claim rewards." is answered for any character outside `SquadUtil::CollectPlayerSquad` (:773-777) | The same test (SquadUtil.CollectPlayerSquad); no prisoner source is asked | The C++ asks squad membership only; a prisoner (M6) is not in the player squad |

|---|---|---|

| `getOccupant()` is a hand that is invalid once the occupant is gone | `Fixture.Occupant` unless `FixtureRegistry.IsFree` says it is stale (gone, or no longer held there) | Meitou keeps the field and judges it with IsFree (as its own CageSpots do) |
| The player's cages: each active player squad's `Ownerships` (home furniture of type BF_CAGE, else buildings with that function); `isOwned(handle)`; `isThePlayer()` | One set for the player faction: a cage whose `Owner` is the player's, or in a building the player owns (`FixtureRegistry.OwnsBuilding`: the property's owner, else a fixture's); `isThePlayer` = the owner faction is the player's; listed in registry order | Meitou keeps ownership per faction, not per squad (a bought house's fixtures become the player's on purchase). Only the order of cages can differ from the C++ (which walked squads); it decides the roster order and a match entry's `originalCageIndex` among equally good cages |
| `findAllFurnitureWithFunction(BF_CAGE)` of a building | Its cage fixtures (not its own lock) and the player's cage sites standing in it (`BuildSite.Parent`) | Meitou's furniture is either an interior fixture or a player site with a parent |

| `inWhat.getBuilding()` | The fixture `CharacterCold.InWhat` names, when one is registered (null: not known, kept as the C++ keeps an unknown cage) | Meitou stores the fixture id |
| `isChainedMode()` (LeaderboardStore, PrisonerUtil 6.2) | `LockSystem.Chained` (wears a piece with a lock) | Meitou's chained flag (CAP 15) |

| `CollectNearbyCagePrisoners` clears the caller's vector | The port's callers append (their lists are fresh); the discovery fills its own list and appends it | The Core/Glue hosts' contract (task 4.5a / 4.6b) |
| Between `PrepareMatch` and the cage return and lock | Closed by task 6.3: the match's end, a cancelled ingress and the window's Stop run the return, which clears the match state as the C++ (no entry, handlers restored, protection stopped, `HasRuntimeActivity` false) | (Was interim until task 6.3) |

(41 % 8 = 1) as an unseeded CRT on a thread nobody else used would. Line order and texts are the C++'s |

| `IssueLiftOrder` (:768-784) | Task 6.3 (its only callers are the return's carry) | Ported with the carrying it starts (section below) |
| Who the protection breaks off (`ProtectMatchPrisoners`: every attacker that is not the prisoner's sparring opponent; before the spar runs `IsSparringOpponent` is false for everyone, so the coming opponent counts as an outsider in the C++ too, SparSession.cpp:515-540) | The same rule. Measured live (prisoner-release.sh, 2026-11-15, Scratch; pg prisoners' test-only outsider ids): 29 break-offs in 364 passes, all on Scratch's town guards (Scratch Gate Guard c:102/152/153/154, Scratch Warlord Captain and Guards c:266/267/268/270, 1-8 each) going for the released Dust Bandit (a Bloodraider), plus the squad member the scenario ordered to attack; never the opponent, the handler or another squad member. These are outsiders in the C++ as well (a hostile prisoner is fought by the normal rules, PA §2.8), so the break-offs match it. Difference: in Kenshi the attackTarget / iShouldntAggravateThisTarget hooks refused such an attack before it began; in Meitou these guards still take the bandit as a target by a route the task 3.2b postfixes (BestAttackerOf / FindFoe, which includes the trouble-maker search, AggroSystem.cs:1798) do not cover (not identified: an arrest or town-duty engage), and are broken off by the 0.10 s protection, so a guard may begin an attack before it is stopped. An earlier run counted 41 break-offs before the scenario's own order (counts vary with where the guards stand) | Meitou's guards engage outside the AI foe choice; the protection, as in the C++, is what ends it |

## PrisonerUtil (task 6.3, ringside aid, return and lock)

`Core/Glue/PrisonerUtilLogic.Return.cs` (the C++ decisions of PrisonerUtil.cpp:111-113, 117-119, 539-744, 768-784, 1311-1368, 1407-1774, fake-tested
in `Tests/Glue/PrisonerUtilReturnTests.cs`), glue `src/Game/PrisonerUtil.Return.cs` (the host's engine half), adapters `src/Engine/Cages.cs`
(PutInCage, IsDestroyed, IsBroken, HasLock, IsLocked) and `src/Engine/PgEngine.cs` (IsCarrying, BeginLift, LetGo). Every timer (3 s, 20 s, 2.5 s,
60 s, 30 s) runs on PgClock (sim ms) through the frame loop's PrisonerUtil.TickReturn / TickRingsideAid slots (ArenaUI.cpp:3157, :3167).

| Original | In this port | Why / effect |
|---|---|---|
| `prisoner->setPrisonMode(true, cage)` (FinishCageEntry), then `ChooseCageLockAction` asks the handler to lock it: `checkPlayerOrderForProblems(LOCK_DOOR, cage)`, `addOrder(cage, LOCK_DOOR, ...)`, and verifies with KenshiLib's `AI::isDoorLocked` for up to 30 s | `world.Fixtures.Imprison(world, prisoner, cage)` (FixtureRegistry.cs:1593-1640; spike S11), which locks the cage itself (lock level from the cage record, 75 for the Prisoner Cage); a carried prisoner is let go first (`CarrySystem.LetGo`), one already in that cage is left as it is, one held in another cage is released there first. The verification reads `LockState.EffectivelyLocked` (locked, neither the lock nor its cage broken, level at least 1), so a working cage is Secured on the first check, right after the placement. No lock order is given (`LockOrderRejected` is never true and `IssueLockOrder` does nothing): the state machine and its states are the C++'s, the IssueOrder state is a pass-through to Wait | Meitou has no lock order for one cage (its cage locking is a warden's round, LockSystem.Rounds.cs:520) and Imprison has locked it already. Effects: the C++'s 1.3 "cage left unlocked" case cannot happen; the handler never walks to the cage to lock it (in the C++ the handler's lock walk took a few seconds after the placement); a cage whose lock does not count as locked after Imprison (a broken lock, a level-0 lock) waits the 30 s and fails with "native lock did not engage before timeout (blocked or broken lock)", the C++ text for that case. "native cage lock order was rejected" cannot happen (kept in the code, unreachable); "native cage lock verification is unavailable" happens only without a prisoner and handler AI to ask (the C++'s `verify && ai`; the port's verifier always exists). Every other failure text ("destination cage or pole is missing or destroyed", "... does not contain the expected prisoner", "... has no operable lock", "... is broken", "no conscious handler can lock the destination") is reached as in the C++ |
| `setPrisonMode(true)` keeps whatever sentence the prisoner had | Imprison books a new sentence from the prisoner's bounty for the cage owner's faction (none for a prisoner without one), as every Meitou caging does; no carrier is named, so no bounty claim is raised | Meitou's Imprison is the one caging call (S11); a player cage's faction rarely books a bounty on its own prisoner |
| `prisoner->setChainedMode(true, hand(prisoner))` after the placement | Nothing | As task 6.2's release: Meitou's chained state is wearing a piece with a lock; the mod puts no shackles on (an item change). A prisoner that came out shackled stays shackled, one that did not stays free in its cage |
| `cage->isBroken()`, `building->destroyed` | `Machine.Broken` of the cage (the save's `broken`, UseableStuff +0x3b6, set by every dismantle step; mirrored as `LockState.OwnerBroken`) and `BuildSite.Destroyed` of its player site; a town cage is never "destroyed" (its ruin unregisters it, which reads as missing) | The same flags Meitou keeps for those Kenshi fields |
| `handler->addOrder(NULL, LIFT_PERSON_PLAYER_ORDER, prisoner, ...)` (IssueLiftOrder) | `CarrySystem.BeginPickUp(world, handler, prisoner, Idle, source Goal)` (row LiftPersonPlayerOrder): the pick-up task walks to the body and lifts it; asked again every 2.5 s until carried, as the C++ | Meitou's lift entry (CAP 12, spike S8). The task 3.2b BeginPickUp refusal (an outsider lifting an isolated match prisoner) lets the mod's own carrier through: the published combat view lists each match prisoner with its handler and `CombatMatchView.IsOwnCarrier` exempts that pair (one mechanism; M8's aftercare carriers go there too). The pair is published for the whole match, not only the return, so a handler may also lift its own released prisoner mid-fight, which the 3.2b refusal stopped before: closer to the C++, which refused no pick-up |
| `dropCarriedObject(false, false)` before the placement | `CarrySystem.LetGo` (the body let go where the carrier stands, no put-down knock-out) | Meitou's put-in-cage path (the arrest system's) |
| `clearAllAIGoals()` + `reThinkCurrentAIAction()` (the return's start; ringside aid's first order) | `TaskStart.End(Stopped)` (PgEngine.EndTask: the task and its walk ended, a new decision asked), as FightStarter's cleanup. Port only: the character leaves FightStarter's stale-target sweep first (as before every move since task 6.2), also before a lift or a treat order | Meitou has no AI goal list for a player character; the sweep (task 3.5) would stop a handler it still watches |
| `ArenaMedical::IssueStabilizeOrder(handler, prisoner)` (FIRST_AID_ORDER or FIRST_AID_ROBOT, whichever need is higher) | M4's `ArenaMedical.IssueStabilizeOrder` (TaskStart.Start Treat with the first-aid or robot row; Meitou's treat task picks the kit each part needs) | One treat path (task 4.7); the robot rule is ArenaMedicalLogic's, tested there |
| `ou->showPlayerAMessage(message, true)` for each "Could not secure ..." | `world.Post(message)` (PgEngine.Message) | As every serial-step message of the port (task 2.5) |
| The walk back of a released prisoner (outsiders kept off by ProtectMatchPrisoners while `returning`) | The same rule; measured live (prisoner-return.sh, 2026-11-15, Scratch): during the bandit's 35 s walk from the arena to its cage the protection broke off 86 outsider attacks in one run (counts vary with where the guards stand), all Scratch town guards (Scratch Gate Guard c:152 / c:154 about 40 each), the bandit was never knocked down and walked straight in (2,660 -> 0 from the cage) | Task 6.2's note: these guards take a hostile prisoner as a target by a route task 3.2b's isolation does not cover; the 0.10 s protection stops each attack, as the C++'s protection would. Not widened here (no new patch in 6.3) |

## Prisoner matches without a cage release (task 9.3d)

Kenshi's `setPrisonMode(false, NULL)` always exists and always frees (ReleasePrisonerFromCage, PrisonerUtil.cpp:746-766, marks the entry
released right after it). The port's release is Meitou's `FixtureRegistry.Release`, bound by reflection (`Members.FixturesRelease`, part
CageRelease) because 0.20.2 added a parameter. Two port-only rules follow (`Core/Glue/PrisonerUtilLogic.Discovery.cs` PrepareMatch,
`Core/Glue/PrisonerUtilLogic.Release.cs` ReleasePrisonerFromCage):

| Original | In this port | Why |
|---|---|---|
| PrepareMatch refuses only for a pending return / lock, too few handlers or a prisoner without a cage | Also when the engine cannot release a prisoner (`Cages.ReleaseAvailable` false: neither Release signature found): "Prisoner matches unavailable: this game version cannot release prisoners from cages" (port-only text, no C++ counterpart), checked after the state is cleared and only for a match with a prisoner; matches without prisoners start as before | otherwise the match would start with the prisoner still caged and locked. Never seen: every Meitou version 0.17.3-0.20.2 has one of the two signatures (docs/versions.md) |
| The entry is marked released after `setPrisonMode(false)` whatever happened | After `LeavePrison` the logic reads `IsImprisoned`: a prisoner still held (the release refused, or a throw read as refused) stays unreleased and pending, without the release line; the unlock tick tries again next tick, and the pair is not sent to the arena | the port's release can refuse; Kenshi's cannot. A freed prisoner behaves exactly as before |

## Prisoner recruitment (task 6.4a, freedom contracts: rules and recruiting)

`Core/Glue/PrisonerRecruitmentGlue.cs` (every function of PrisonerRecruitment.cpp, fake-tested in `Tests/Glue/PrisonerRecruitmentGlueTests.cs`) and
`Core/Glue/PrisonerRecruitmentUILogic.cs` (every non-widget decision and text of PrisonerRecruitmentUI.cpp, `Tests/Glue/PrisonerRecruitmentUILogicTests.cs`),
glue `src/Game/PrisonerRecruitment.cs` and the sim half `src/Game/PrisonerRecruitmentUISim.cs` (the frame loop's PrisonerRecruitmentUI.Tick slot,
ArenaUI.cpp:3171), the recruit adapter `src/Engine/Recruiting.cs` (part Recruitment: Members.JoinPlayer / PlayerHeads, internal). The window half
(widgets, layouts, portraits) is task 6.4b.

| Original | In this port | Why / effect |
|---|---|---|
| `ou->player->recruit(prisoner, false)` (RecruitAction::Apply), then `setPrisonMode(false, NULL)` | The recruit (Recruiting.Recruit): the faction cap first (`DialogueSystem.PlayerHeads(world)` below CONSTANTS `max faction size`, internal), the prisoner let out of its cage (`FixtureRegistry.Release`, releaser -1: no crime, not escaped), then internal `DialogueSystem.JoinPlayer(world, slot, talker)` by reflection (DialogueSystem.Recruit.cs:93-131: faction, squad, IsPlayer, AI and jobs cleared, Save = null). The release is BEFORE the join and is the prison mode's end | Meitou has no public recruit; its own recruits check the cap and call JoinPlayer, releasing a caged one first (GiveSlaves :157-160, CAP 15: JoinPlayer does not release). The cap check is what Meitou's recruit does where Kenshi's own recruit is the C++'s: at the cap the recruit fails with the C++ "Kenshi could not add this prisoner to the current squad; the payment was cancelled." |
| A failed native recruit leaves the prisoner as it was | A JoinPlayer that refuses after the release (no active squad) puts the prisoner back in the cage it came from (`Cages.PutInCage`: Imprison, which locks the cage) | The release had to come first; a refused recruit still leaves a prisoner, as the C++'s. The cage is locked again even if it was not (a caged roster prisoner's cage is locked in practice) |
| JoinPlayer's `talker` | The first living player character in table order (slot in use, a player, never died; the count DialogueSystem.PlayerHeads makes) | Kenshi's recruit has no talker; JoinPlayer does not read it in 0.17.3 |
| `ou && ou->player` (the "player faction is not ready" refusal) | The world's DialogueSystem exists and the player has a living character (the talker) | What the recruit needs in Meitou |
| `setChainedMode(false, hand(prisoner))` after a recruit | Nothing | As task 6.2's release: Meitou's chained state is wearing a piece with a lock (no flag to clear) |
| (no counterpart) the recruit's members missing | Part Recruitment off (one log line "recruitment off: ..."); `Recruit` refuses after the C++ checks, before anything is reserved, with the port's "This game version cannot add a prisoner to your squad (the recruitment part is off; see the log)."; the window's button, where the C++ rules would enable it, reads "Recruitment Unavailable" with that explanation (a Marks deficit or a busy arena is still shown as the C++ shows it) | The C++ always has Kenshi's recruit, so it has no text for it (new text, task brief) |
| `OnRecruitClicked` recruits `g_selected` inside the click | The window sends `RecruitPrisoner(prisoner, seq, shownPrice)`; the sim runs it only while the window still selects that prisoner at that price, else not at all (no status change, a debug line, the model's LastOfferChanged); a command above the model's LastSeq is recorded as LastSeq, one at or below it is dropped (the window numbers its requests above LastSeq; the test verb does too) | A command is applied a tick later; a second click before the first one's result, or a roster change, must not recruit someone at a price nobody was shown (as 5.2b's purchase re-check) |
| An exception in the recruitment (no C++ counterpart) | Never escapes. Inside the transaction: whether the recruit happened is read back (Apply returned true, or it threw and the character is now a player character); a recruit that did not happen cancels the reservation (the C++ "could not add" text); one that did is never undone or refunded: its payment is accepted then (a success, the roster prisoner forgotten), and if that fails the C++ "Recruitment failed and its payment could not be restored. Check Arena Marks before saving.", still forgotten from the roster (the reservation is not held: Accept false = not pending). In the engine half a throw from the release or the join is read back (RecruitingRules): a player = joined, otherwise put back in its cage, that checked (an outcome and a log line when it fails); when that read throws too, it is never caged (it may be a player now) and the recovery reads it again. A throw from a step after the paid recruit (forget, name, debug line) is logged and skipped: the success stands. A throw in a read before anything moved: the "could not be restored" text; a Meitou error line each | As RewardDelivery (task 5.2b review): the ledger is never left reserved by an exception it can undo |
| A failed rollback (`AccountingRollbackFailed`) | Also a Meitou error line naming the prisoner and the price | As RewardDelivery's failed rollbacks |
| PrisonerRecruitmentUI::Tick refreshes every frame while visible | Every sim tick while visible (the frame-loop slot); a new model only when the view changed | The sim half runs per tick (overview §4.2) |

## Prisoner recruitment window, window half (task 6.4b)

<!-- task 6.4b block -->
`src/Ui/PrisonerRecruitmentUI.cs` (PrisonerRecruitmentScreen), the pixel arithmetic and the request numbering in
`Core/Glue/PrisonerRecruitmentUILayout.cs` (tested in `Tests/Glue/PrisonerRecruitmentUILayoutTests.cs`), the window framework's `PgScreen.Failed`
hook (`src/Ui/ScreenHost.cs`).

| Original | In this port | Why / effect |
|---|---|---|
| EnsureWindow builds the widgets once, RebuildRows the rows whenever the candidates change; every RefreshAll (each frame while visible) recaptions them and runs LayoutWindow (Show, a new candidate list, a new client size or body height) or else LayoutRows + LayoutDetails | The XML layout is rebuilt and `Replace`d only when the row count changes (the shape key; the rows are named by their index, as the C++); within a shape a plan made once per layout binds every rectangle (as fractions of its parent), the captions, the detail portrait's visibility, the action button's enabled state, the selected row, the canvases and the window rectangle. The LayoutWindow rule is kept: the status line and the two scroll views are sized for the status text of the last LayoutWindow, so a refused recruit's text keeps the old rectangles as in the C++ | An XML layout is fixed until it is replaced |
| `NativeUI::RowHeight(g_recruit, 0)` uses the button's caption size and font | The caption's standard-text height and the standard font height | As every window since 4.5b (one measure) |
| Passive (:80-91) takes mouse and key focus off the rows' portrait, name and Marks and the detail portrait | NeedMouse false on them: a press there reaches the row button, which sends the same RecruitmentSelect | MyGuiScreenHost activates only the picked widget; same effect |
| BindPortrait hides the portrait only without a PortraitManager | Hidden also when no portrait cell is free (a full squad, spike S7) | No portrait API in Meitou (CAP 7.4) |
| OnRecruitClicked recruits `g_selected` inside the click | The button sends RecruitPrisoner(selection, sequence, shown price); the sequence is above the model's LastSeq (`RecruitmentRequestGate`); nothing is sent while the last request is unanswered (the button stays enabled); a new world starts the numbers over | Window / sim split (task 6.4a: the sim drops a request at or below LastSeq) |
| A click acts on the widgets as they are at that moment (one thread) | Every window's click is judged against the model the window was last bound (drawn) with, not the newest published one (task 6.4b review, `ScreenFrame.ClickModel`): a row click names the prisoner that row showed, Grant Freedom carries the price shown; the sim half re-checks both against its state now (6.4a: another selection or price is not run). The same holds for the Rewards window's rows and claims (the offer the card showed) and the Arena window's rows | The model can change between the frame the player saw and the click (window / sim split) |
| Escape closes the recruitment window in ArenaUI's order (ArenaUI.cpp:2994-2997); the title bar's close button calls Close (:480-484) | Meitou's own Escape closes the top window and its close button closes it, both reported as `Closed`; the sim half then acts as Close (the Arena window comes back) | As the other windows (4.6a) |
| A failed EnsureWindow logs "Proving Grounds: prisoner recruitment UI creation failed at <resource>: <what>" and Show returns: no window, the Arena window stays hidden; the next Show tries again | The framework's failed Register calls the window's `Failed`: the same line (the skin being made when the layout builder threw, else the last one EnsureWindow names, Kenshi_Button1; one line per open request, as the C++ logs one per Show); the framework itself reports the window `Closed` when the sim half asked it open (every window, since the 6.4b review), so the sim half stops thinking it visible and the Arena window comes back. The next "Recruit Prisoners" (a new open request) builds and registers it again, as the C++'s next Show does (the framework retries a failed layout on a new open request or a new shape, for every window: each C++ window creates its widgets again at its next show). A failed Replace (the rows) logs the C++ "Proving Grounds: prisoner recruitment UI refresh failed" and the framework closes the window and reports it | The window is built at interface load, not at Show; the sim half holds the visibility |

No reference picture shows the recruitment window (ws-01 to ws-15 hold none), so the live picture
`_shots/provinggrounds/recruit-window.png` (`tools/scenarios/prisoner-recruit.sh`, 1920 x 1080, font height 26) was compared with the C++
coordinates only: the 88 % window centred, the subtitle panel on top, the PRISONERS heading over the roster column (32 % of the width), each row a
button with its portrait (3 bodies), name and Marks, the selected row lit; the detail column with the portrait (7 bodies), name, stats line and
explanation; the status line above the two buttons (Back to Arena as wide as its caption + 4 gaps, the action button the rest). What differs:
runs of spaces drawn as one ("CONTRACTS OF FREEDOM | Prisoners ...", "Combat 14 | 77 / 40 Arena Marks"; the captions carry the C++'s two
spaces) and the 26 px text are the renderer's (as task 4.8 / 5.3b). `recruit-window-after-refusal.png` shows the forced grant's refusal still on the
status line: the C++ keeps `g_message` until a row is selected or the window is shown again (RefreshAll(true)); `recruit-window.png` is taken after
the row is clicked (Select clears it).

## TownArena (task 7.1, when Scratch is active, the schedule clock, the stop rules)

`Core/Glue/TownArenaLogic.cs` + `TownArenaLogic.Scheduling.cs` (the C++ decisions of TownArena.cpp:56-134, 368-428, 620-632, 1296-1359,
1405-1414, 1457-1462, 1781-1858 and Tick's stop rules and schedule clock, :1860-1966; fake-tested in `Tests/Glue/TownArenaLogicTests.cs`), glue

| Original | In this port | Why / effect |
|---|---|---|
| `getObjectsWithinSphere(pos, 2500, BUILDING, 512)` returns buildings in the engine's order; BindRegistryNear binds the first Town Registry of the player's town | `PgEngine.BuildingsNear`: the placed towns' buildings first (in `PopulationData.Sites` order), then the player's construction, 3D distance, at most 512 | Meitou has no such engine query. Scratch holds one Town Registry (census), so the order cannot change which registry is bound there; with two finished Town Registries of one town in reach the port may bind another one than the original would |
| `getCurrentTownLocation()` / `Building::getTown()` | Since the task 7.2 review: `Towns.CurrentTownOf` (the status town, see "Matchmaking snapshot and search worker") and `PgBuilding.Town` (TownBuildingBuilder.MemberOf / BuildSite.Town), compared as Core `TownKey`s. (Until then `Towns.TownAt`, Meitou's `AiSystem.TownAt` = FUN_1402688d0, the AI blackboard's town: wrongly called the getCurrentTownLocation rule here; that function is FUN_1405c89b0, the status town.) | CAP 6 / task 2.2; the same test as the C++ (equal and neither NULL) |
| The world's AbandonWorldState chain (GameWorld clear) calls `TownArena::AbandonWorldState` | The system is rebuilt for every world (its state is new); the old world's TownArena runs `AbandonWorldState` when a new world takes over (`ProvingGroundsSystem.TakeOver`): its subsystem resets (placeholders today) and, when an event ran, `ArenaMedical::SetProtocol(previous)` | The protocol is process-wide and must not carry into the next world (ArenaMedical.cs header). As in the C++ order (WorldLifecycle.cpp:265, :267) the old world's SparSession is abandoned first (`Core/Glue/OldWorldAbandon.cs`, each step guarded), so `SetProtocol` is not refused by a match still active in the old world. A world closed without a new one (back to the title) runs no AbandonWorldState until the next world's TakeOver does it |
| `ou->isPaused()` stops the schedule clock | never true in a serial step (a paused world runs no SlowWorld); the clock is `PgClock.NowMs` (sim ms, frozen while paused) | As every port timer (ArenaIngress, PrisonerUtil). At high game speed the 5 s clock covers more game time, as TW §2.1 notes for the original |
| Nothing between a manual save's preparation and the save (the C++ prepares and saves in one call) | Port only: while `WorldLifecycle.SaveInFlight` (a save the sim prepared waits for the frame thread, or a manual save waits for cage locks) Tick's idle branch plans and begins nothing (after the player-in-town check, before PlanNpcBout) | Closes WorldLifecycle.cs's TO-DO M7: a bout must not start between the cancel and the capture. Effect: the next schedule check after the save does it |
| `kShowTownStatusPopups` is false (TownArena.cpp:118): status changes only go to pg_debug.log | The same (Core `TownArena.UpdateStatus`; nothing shown) | Kept |
| No "Scratch is active" readout | Port only: the F10 status row's TownArena phase (`active in Scratch` / the event phase / `idle`, then the status text) and the test-only `pg town` | Diagnostics; no behaviour |

Collect sorts its npcs and players by id and its maps by key, so only the medics' order (and which characters a cap of 512 would drop: the census counts 311 at most) follows the slot order |

Session-only, as in the C++ (the key distinguishes namesakes and goes stale on reload) |
| Ability reads `getMeleeAttack_melee()`, `getMeleeAttack_unarmed(true)`, `getMeleeDefence_melee(false)`, `getDodge(true)`, `strengthActual()`, `dexterityActual()`, `toughness()`, `getEquippedWeaponSkill()` | Meitou's documented ports of those getters: `CombatSystem.AttackRating` (FUN_140884ee0), `UnarmedRating` (FUN_140884e00, flag set), `DefenceRating(guard: false)` (FUN_140886290, flag clear), all internal and bound by reflection (`Members.cs`, part CombatProfile); the dodge = the HUD's Dodge line (FUN_140885190, internal `HudTips.AddDodge`, recomputed from public reads, unrounded, the bool read as "with the defensive-mode term"); `strengthActual` / `dexterityActual` = `CombatSystem.Eff` for those stats (stat x scale x `MedicalState.StatMultiplier`, no gear term); `toughness()` = `ScaledToughness`; weapon skills = the stored levels, `getEquippedWeaponSkill` = the stored level of the weapon-in-hand's skill | Meitou does not name these functions; the mapping is by the functions its sources say it ports. Which Kenshi getter each FUN_ address is, and the meaning of `getDodge`'s flag, are not settled by the sources (INFERRED). A missing member makes every profile unusable (ability -1, one log line) |
| `getPrimaryWeapon()`, else `getSecondaryWeapon()` when the primary is missing, invalid or unarmed | `Fighter.BackWeapon`, else `Fighter.HipWeapon` (Meitou's two weapon slots, in the order `Fighter.Weapon` prefers them); arms not consulted, as the C++ | Which Kenshi slot is "primary" is not documented in Meitou (INFERRED: the back slot, the one drawn first). A character with no medical or combat state (`CharacterCold.Medical` / `Fighter` null; none among people) reads as an unusable profile |
| Ready's `isInCombatMode(true, true)` | `CharacterCold.InCombat` (the fight flag, melee or ranged) | Meitou's own `IsInCombatMode` (internal) also asks the ranged controller's target to be up; the flag alone reads "in combat" a little longer after a shooting target went down |
| A medic's native capacity: `!isInBloodlossTrauma() && !isProbablyDying() && !isCrippled() && hasAnArmToFightWith()` | `!MedicalState.BloodKo && !Crawling && (ArmWorks(left) \|\| ArmWorks(right))`; `isProbablyDying` reads false | Meitou has no "probably dying" state (its sources name no counterpart). Medics are counted only for capacity (M8 aftercare) |
| UpdateMatchmakingPreview (and NPC planning) read the roster as soon as the town is loaded | Port only (census rule 3): `UpdatePreviewRoster` returns as "not now" until `RosterSettled`: the bound Town Registry's town has had a player character for 10 s (a settle clock checked once a second in Tick, stopped while the town is empty). Task 7.3 asks the same before planning | The town fills about 10 s after the player arrives (census: 2 characters and no fixtures at 3 s). A search on the half-empty roster would answer every card "No suitable recovered opponents" until the next 10 s refresh. Effect: the cards start searching up to ~11 s after arrival |
| One worker thread per process (file-scope globals), started on first use, `Shutdown` from `TownArena::AbandonWorldState`; it dies with the process | One worker per world (`TownArena` owns it), started on first use, shut down by the old world's `AbandonWorldState` when the next world takes over (no thread outlives its world), a background thread with a process-exit handler; back at the title screen the idle worker waits (holding only copied requests) until the next world's TakeOver or the exit | Meitou rebuilds the systems for every world (task 7.1). Same queue, generation and lock rules (Core.TownMatchmakingSearch.Queue, one lock per call, the search outside it) |
| A search cannot throw (the C++ has no exceptions there) | Port only: a search that throws completes as an invalid match (the card's "no suitable opponents" path), logged once | An exception on a .NET thread would end the game process |
| `Character::getCurrentTownLocation()` (vfunc 0x78 = FUN_1405c89b0) reads the status object's current town `+0x88`, which FUN_1405a51e0 writes in five steps (Meitou docs/game/carry-loot.md "The status town": 1 the player faction's town within 1.25 x radius; 2 the current town within 1.5 x radius, or with none the nearest town (of any kind but a nest marker) within 1 x; 3 a walled town's point within 300; 4 the nearest player town, step 2's factor; 5 the town of the building the character stands in) at each AI update; whatever it finds is the current town of the next update | `Towns.CurrentTownOf` (src/Engine/Towns.cs, rule in Core `TownRule.CharacterTown`, unit-tested): Meitou's copy `CharacterCold.StatusTown` (ArrestSystem keeps it with `AiSystem.StatusTownAt`, steps 1, 2, 4, on its spotting pass) wins. When it is none, the port keeps its own stand-in for `+0x88` (a per-character memo, `StatusTownMemo`, per world): `AiSystem.StatusTownAt` (internal, `Members.AiStatusTownAt`) is asked with the memo as the current town (none: what Meitou's first update would write, entering at 1 x; a memo: step 2 holds it to 1.5 x), then step 5, the town of the building whose indoor volume holds the character (`world.Sight.BuildingOf` -> `PgBuilding.Town`); the answer becomes the memo (none clears it), and a town Meitou's StatusTown gives drops it. Without the member: the memo while within 1.5 x its radius (`TownRule.Holds`), else the 1 x radius rule (one log line). Used by TownArena (the player-in-town checks, the registry's town, Collect) and the Marks HUD's "in Scratch" | Meitou does not model step 3 (a walled town's point) or step 5, never stores a step-5 town in StatusTown, and its StatusTown is none at load until the first update (CharacterTable.cs:1038-1042) and updated on the spotting pass, not each AI update (cadence Unknown in the original). The memo restores step 5's town and its 1.5 x hold as the original keeps them in `+0x88` (a fighter that spawned in its Scratch house keeps Scratch out to 3,750 from the town point); steps 1, 2, 4 stay before 5 as in the original. Remaining effect: step 3's walled-town reach is missing; a memo of a character gone stays until the world ends. The memo is written only when the port reads a character's town (the original rewrites `+0x88` on every status pass): a fighter that left its building before the port first read it (the window between loading a save, or the town becoming active, and the first read) never gets Scratch through step 5; an NPC read only every 10 s (the preview's refresh) may keep a 1.5 x hold the original would have dropped (it left and came back between two reads); the test verbs (`pg town roster / search`) read, and so move, it too |
| `RosterStatus::Read` writes its reason through Ready's pointer | The same text through `out` (Core RosterStatus.Read); `hasMedical` = the character has a medical state | Copy |

Measured live (task 7.2 review 2, `tools/scenarios/town-roster.sh`, base list, about a minute after a new game in Scratch): the 2,500 circle round
the registry holds 177 catalog fighters (the census's 177). By the status town (`Towns.CurrentTownOf`, with the memo) 164 are in Scratch and 13
are in no town (with the earlier 1 x radius rule, `Towns.TownAt`: 160 and 17). The 13 (squad 226: Mace 230 x 2, 232 x 5, Hacker hard 679 x 6) stand
outdoors 1,126-1,200 from Scratch's town point (well inside its 2,500 radius), but Fallen Tower's centre (site 252, radius 875) is nearer, 948-1,035
away: the status rule (step 2 with no current town) tests only the nearest town, which does not reach them; they are not indoors (no step 5), have
no StatusTown from Meitou and no memo. This is the original's nearest-town rule, so they are not town opponents in the original either unless they
were placed in Scratch before (indoors at spawn: the memo now covers that). The census faction filter left out 6 characters of record
`94-Proving Grounds.mod` (not Bloodstamp; no catalog, medic or named record). All 170 town candidates (164 fighters + the 6 named) and the 10 medics
were ready; nothing was rejected; 0 ambiguous identity keys. town-active: the squad teleported 3,500 west of the registry stands 4,266 from Scratch's
town point (beyond the 1.5 x 2,500 = 3,750 hold), and the stop rule fired as before.

Meitou's own log of that run was overwritten by the next run of the same scenario (live.sh keeps one `live-<scenario>.log`), so what Meitou did after reading the mod data is not known. Not reproduced: town-challenge run 2 (autosave1) and run 4 (autosave2) loaded the same kind of save; `tools/scenarios/town-autosave-load.sh` (an autosave with no booking, then one with an idle booking, each quickloaded, a hang keeps the screen state and the log ends in `hang-*.txt`) ran 3 times: 6 of 6 loads came back (2026-11-16) | Open: to settle at the M7 gate. If it comes back, `town-autosave-load.sh` keeps the evidence; candidates: Meitou's own loader (the mod's decode had finished), or the hold / booking state, which is not saved (the C++'s session state) |

## Hold registry (task 8.4a)

`Core/Glue/HoldRegistry.cs` (fake-tested in `Tests/Glue/HoldRegistryTests.cs`), engine half `src/Engine/Holds.cs` (one object per world, TownArena's).
Port only: the C++ has no counterpart object.

| Original | In this port | Why / effect |
|---|---|---|
| An NPC's AI is taken over through its order receiver in one of TownAftercare's reservation tables (medics, patients, bench, external), which the checkOrders hook reads (TownAftercare.cpp:29-33, :97-115); a town fighter by clearAllAIGoals at each move order (ArenaIngress.cpp:437); a table entry is removed by its owner | One owner-counted hold list per world: `Hold(id, owner)` holds an NPC by spike S10's recipe (`Engaged = true`, `Ai.Walking = false`, `TaskStart.Stand` once) the first time anyone holds it; a later owner only adds itself (and re-asserts `Engaged`); `Release(id, owner)` gives the NPC back (`Engaged = false; Ai.Replan = true`) only when no owner is left. Owners: town bout fighter, aftercare medic, aftercare patient (8.4b), bench, external, spectator (8.2). One re-assert pass per SlowWorld (TownArena.Tick, where task 7.3 re-asserted its borrow list); a stale id is dropped there; the list is forgotten on every world change (CAP 19: Engaged is not saved). TownArena's borrow list and the Teams 1v1 bench (PgSparHost) moved onto it; KOWatcher no longer re-asserts the bench itself | Meitou has no checkOrders hook; `Engaged` is the hold (spike S10). M7's three separate lists each released with an unconditional `Engaged = false`, which would hand back an NPC another subsystem still holds (task 7.3's TO-DO: Finish's release freed a fighter the aftercare holds as a patient). With owners, Finish's release of the bout fighters leaves a patient held. M4's Park keeps its own list (it skips NPCs TownArena holds as fighters; no overlap seen live) |
| (no C++ counterpart: a known overlap) | M4's Park keeps its own Engaged list: it skips only NPCs the registry holds as TownFighter, and its release (`ReleaseParked`) sets `Engaged = false` unconditionally, so a Park release could clear Engaged on an NPC the registry holds for another owner (Bench, AftercareMedic) until the next SlowWorld's re-assert pass puts it back | Left as it is (controller decision 8.4a: Park stays unless a live run shows an overlap; none seen). Park parks only staging fighters, which are not medics; a bench NPC is a player-arena fighter Park may also park |
| A player character in a reservation table: its receiver is reserved like an NPC's (the hook then reports its orders handled) | A player character is reserved in the table (the C++'s refusals and capacities apply to it) but never held: `Holds.Hold` answers whether it exists and records nothing | The AI never runs a player character (AiSystem.cs:366); Engaged on one would do nothing useful |

## TownAftercare (task 8.4c, bed recovery and the treatment record) and ArenaMedical's restock

`Core/Glue/TownAftercareLogic.Recovery.cs` (TownAftercare.cpp:371-402, :1035-1050, :1227-1292; fake-tested in
`Tests/Glue/TownAftercareRecoveryTests.cs`), glue `src/Town/TownAftercare.Recovery.cs`, bed orders `src/Engine/Beds.cs` (LeaveBedMode,
StopOperating, OfTownRecord); ArenaMedical.cpp:73-155, :167-199 in `src/Game/ArenaMedical.cs` (the Apply prefix and a new postfix),
`src/Engine/Treatments.cs` and `ArenaMedicalLogic.RestockMedicIfDepleted` (fake-tested in `Tests/Glue/MedicRestockTests.cs`).

| Original | In this port | Why / effect |
|---|---|---|
| Bed entries learned by the `Character::setBedMode` hook (RecordBedEntry :75-84): anyone who lies down in a hospital-163 bed, by any route, goes into the bed registry at that moment | Entries learned by scan: TickBedRecovery's 1 s poll first reads every bed fixture of every standing hospital 163 of Scratch's site (found again when Scratch's site or the world's ruins revision changes, a building destroyed or rebuilt, and every 30 s while none) and remembers each non-player occupant lying in it (RememberBedOccupant's rules and 64-slot cap; a known occupant keeps its clock unless it moved to another bed); 8.4a's adoption and 8.4b's delivery poll still add theirs. An entry is seen up to one poll (1 s) after the character lay down. Beds of a hospital 163 outside Scratch are not scanned (there is none in the census) | Meitou has no setBedMode hook. The 600 s stay clock starts at the first poll that sees the occupant, so a stay lasts at most 1 s longer than the C++'s. Anyone the town AI puts in those beds is covered like the C++ (a healthy NPC who lies down there is taken out at the next poll, as the C++ would). Characters already lying in a hospital bed when a world loads (a save) are adopted by the first poll, their clock starting then: the C++'s hook saw no entry for them (a load is not a setBedMode call) until a Begin adopted them; not gated, because gating would leave patients from an earlier session in the beds for good |
| `ReleaseBedOccupant` (:376-402): `setBedModeOriginal(p, false, bed)`; if the character is still `IN_BED`, `bed->stopOperating(actor)`; then, if it left and is alive, `TownMedicAIPolicy::ResumeNative` (clearOrders, reThinkCurrentAIAction, clearAllAIGoals, chooseGoal, callForUpdate) | `setBedMode(false)` = `Beds.LeaveBedMode`: the Sleep task ended (`TaskStart.End(world, slot, TaskEnd.Stopped)`: the bed trip dropped, the task Idle; skipped for a dead character) and at once the engine's own get-up, `FixtureRegistry.GetOutOfBed` (the call `BedSystem.GetUp` makes at its next step: a base bed is left at once, a go-to-bed-clip bed keeps the sleeper while the clip plays backwards); `stopOperating` = `Beds.StopOperating` (`FixtureRegistry.LeaveBed`: out at once, no clip); `ResumeNative` = the hold registry's release of the owner AftercarePatient when the aftercare holds the character (Engaged off + Ai.Replan only once no owner is left: a bout fighter stays held by its TownFighter owner until Finish), else `Ai.Replan = true` (`PgEngine.Replan`) for a character nobody holds; another owner's hold is left to it. The "path" field keeps the C++'s values (set-bed-mode / stop-operating / none) | `LeaveBed` alone leaves the task Sleep, which the AI never holds outside a bed (m8-research.md); ending the task first is the engine's way out. Calling GetOutOfBed now (not at the next BedSystem step) lets the C++'s immediate "still in bed" test mean the same thing. With a go-to-bed-clip bed the fallback ends the clip early (none in Scratch's hospital: every base bed is "sleepinbed") |
| `GetTickCount()` and `ou->isPaused()` (TickBedRecovery :1228-1230) | `PgClock.NowMs` (the sim clock; stops while paused, so the C++'s "paused" test is always false here); the C++'s 1 s step cap kept | As 8.4a's Standby |
| The bed's `hand::toString()` in the release line | The bed fixture id | Diagnostic text only (as 8.4b) |

The aftercare's ten trace slots are filled; nothing else reads them | Meitou has one first-aid step for the three Kenshi calls. A treatment ended by a missing kit or a dead patient is "not complete", as the C++'s false return |

The C++ line byte for byte, and its Error copy for the fallback | Meitou drains an NPC medic's kit only for open wounds and splints (FirstAidSystem.cs:594-595, 631, 685), so the restock can fire only after cut or pierce fights (m8-research.md). Meitou takes a used-up kit out of the inventory at once (RemoveOne, :700), so the fallback recharges an item no longer held: it restores nothing usable (as the C++, it is the error path when no replacement could be given). A splint kit is never restocked (the C++'s applyRigging hook has no restock) |

| (no C++ counterpart) | Port only (per step since the quality review): a throw in one registry entry's step is caught, the entry forgotten (the scan may find it again next poll), logged for the first three ("aftercare bed recovery entry failed actor=... error=...") and counted, so the other entries keep moving; a throw in the sweep itself is logged once per world ("aftercare bed recovery failed") and the sweep runs again at the next tick; each caught aftercare step (release, bed recovery) has its own logged-once flag and count, and Treat's throws are counted too: F10's TownArena phase row adds "aftercare failures: release N, bed recovery N, treat N, bed entries N" once any is above 0, pg town care has `failures`; a throw in the postfix is counted with the prefix's failures (three log lines at most) and the treatment stands. (the chatter, used as it is), glue `src/Town/TownSpectators.cs`, the seat adapter `src/Engine/Seats.cs` (spike S16), the say helper
`src/Town/TownSpeech.cs`.

|---|---|---|

The function is implied by the seat's existence |
| The "nearer this arena" test reads the pillow building's position | It reads the seat's position (the chair stands at the pillow's origin, `TownBuildingBuilder.cs:80-93`) | The same point for a pillow |
| `Occupied(seat)` / `isFreeSlot(actor)` (engine, private), asked per guest each maintenance pass | A seated guest checks its own seat in O(1) (`Seats.SittingOn`: Operate on it and `Operating`); the character table is walked once per maintenance pass only while some guest is not yet seated, for those guests' seats alone, and once at Begin for every pillow seat: occupant = an operator at the seat (task Operate, `OperateSite`, `Operating`) or an AI sitter resting on its place (task Rest + `Ai.SitClip` within 1 unit, the engine's own private test); not free (Begin only) = also an operator on its way or a squad board's sit / seat claim (`Ai.SitClaims` / `SeatClaims`) | The native test is private (m8-research.md "8.2"). Release decisions are the C++'s: a seated guest whose seat anyone takes loses its sit task (Meitou's UseSpecial ends it) and is released as stood up, which the C++'s "taken" test would release too. The cost stays one table walk per pass at most, none once everyone sits |
| `getOccupant()`: one occupant per seat | When an operator and a resting AI sitter are both on a seat, the operator is the occupant whatever their slot order (`SpectatorSeatRules.OccupantWins`); between two of a kind the first slot keeps it | The engine's own operator holds the seat (a resting sitter only stands on its spot); a slot-order rule let a resting neighbour in a lower slot take our sitting guest's seat |
| `addOrder(seat, task, ...)` is silent: a refused order leaves the guest waiting until the 180 s timeout | A refused sit start (`TaskStart.Start` not Started: asleep, fleeing, carried, not eligible) is counted (pg town crowd `sitRefused`), the first of each Begin logged ("spectator sit order refused seat=..."), and the guest released after 2 refusals in a row (3 s apart) | Port only: an NPC whose order Meitou refuses would otherwise stand held, its AI off, for 180 s |
| `couldIOperate(actor)` | `TaskStart.Eligible(world, cold, SitAround)` before any seat is tried | The actor half of the test; a candidate that cannot sit is never invited |
| Invite saves Hold / Passive / the move speed and the seat's task, clears the standing orders and the AI's goals (:128-134); a seated guest gets Passive (:472); ReleaseGuest restores them and resumes the native AI (`ResumeNativeAI`, :82-87, :96-103) | Invite holds the NPC in the world's hold registry with the owner Spectator (spike S10); seated sets no standing order; release = the sit task ended (`TaskStart.End(Stopped)`) and the Spectator hold let go (Engaged off + Ai.Replan when no other owner holds it) | The registry's Engaged is the port's whole AI hand-over (CAP 19); Meitou's AI acts nothing while Engaged, so no Passive is needed; the AI never takes back an Operate task, so the end comes first |
| A deferred release (`DeferNativeResume`: the guest unhealthy) resumes only the native AI later; the guest is no longer controlled meanwhile | The Spectator hold is kept until the guest is healthy (TickPendingReleases lets it go then); a dead or invalid guest's hold goes at once | The C++'s deferral is exactly "do not hand back yet". TickPendingReleases runs from TownArena.Tick after its stop rules, which return early while no player is in Scratch (TownArenaLogic.Scheduling.cs:114, the C++'s order, TownArena.cpp:1885): a deferred guest keeps its hold until a player is back in town (the C++ likewise does not resume it before) |
| `addOrder(seat, task, seat, ...)` + RUN re-issued every 3 s until seated (:473-477) | The sit task (`TaskStart.Start(Operate, SitAround, Npc) { Site, Implicit = true }`, spike S16) walks to the seat at full speed (SpeedMode.Free, the C++'s RUN) and re-paths itself; the port gives it again at the 3 s mark only when the task is no longer under way | Re-starting a running walk every 3 s would reset its path request each time |
| `getPermajobCount() > 0` keeps civilians with a permanent job away (:421) | Meitou's counterpart (`cold.Jobs.Count`) is 0 for every town NPC, so the controller's stand-in is used: a post or duty goal (AiMind.Behaviour Post, Work or Turret while it has a goal; `Ai.TownPost`; an engine mind; a TownDutySystem duty of the character) | Read literally the rule would admit every civilian (shopkeepers and guards at their posts included). The count it leaves out is in pg town crowd (`excludedByDuty`) |
| Candidates: any 103 / 258 / 99 or catalog fighter of the town | Also the town faction 91 (Bloodstamp) only, and never an NPC another owner of the hold registry holds (the aftercare, the external table) | Shared brief (census: with the owner's list other factions walk inside the circle); a held NPC belongs to its owner (task 8.6 takes a cage handler out of the audience through ReleaseCharacter, the other way round) |
| ReleaseCharacter drops a pending entry and leaves the resume to the caller (:345-349) | It also lets the Spectator hold go | The caller's own hold is then the only one; without it the NPC would stay held by nobody's plan |
| `GetTickCount()` (real ms) for dt, the maintenance gate and the chatter seed | `PgClock.NowMs` (sim ms, frozen while paused, scaled by the game speed) | As every town glue: the 180 s timeout and the chatter spacing are game seconds |
