| `getObjectsWithinSphere(arena, 2500, BUILDING, 1024)` + `IsSpot(stringID)` + same town + `isComplete` | `PgEngine.BuildingsNear` filtered to the Announcer Spot record 261 (once per bout), then the C++'s own tests (IsSpot, town, finished, nearest) | The 1024 cap counts only spots (the C++ counts every building in the sphere: Scratch has fewer than 1024 within 2,500) |
| `g_arena` / `g_spot` hands checked with `isValid()` every tick | The refs resolved again (ArenaIdentity.Resolve), cached until the ref changes, a building is ruined or rebuilt (RuinsRevision) or 5 s pass | Resolve builds a PgBuilding record: not on every tick (as the limb shop and the corpse cleanup) |
| The order receiver, `TownAftercare::ReserveExternalOrders` / `ReleaseExternalOrders`, the saved Hold / Passive / speed, `PrepareNativeResume` (`addOrder(MOVE_CUS_ORDERED, position)` + `removeJob` + `clearAllAIGoals` + standing orders + speed) and `reThinkCurrentAIAction` | The character; TownAftercare's external table (4 slots shared with the limb shop and the corpse cleanup: an announcer reservation takes a slot, and a refusal is the C++'s fallback "speaker AI ownership could not be reserved") plus the hold registry's owner External; the resume clears the order queue (`PgEngine.ClearOrders`), puts Hold / Passive / the run speed mode back and replans the AI (`PgEngine.Replan`; the hold's release also replans) | As the medics, the corpse handler and the limb shop |
| `HoldAtStand`: `removeJob`, `clearAllAIGoals`, Hold + Passive, a `STAND_STILL` goal, `lookatPosition(arena)` (every tick once arrived, and at every line) | `PgEngine.EndWalk`, Hold + Passive, the yaw toward the arena (`PgEngine.Face`) written only once the speaker has stopped (`PgEngine.MoveDestination` is null: it reads null when the horizontal velocity is 0); called at every tick as the C++, so the yaw is written once the walk has ended | A held NPC's AI chooses no goal (spike S10); Meitou's movement overwrites a walker's yaw (CAP 5) |
| `IssueMove`: `clearAllAIGoals`, Hold / Passive off, `movement->setDestination` + `setDesiredSpeed(RUN)` + `addOrder(MOVE_CUS_ORDERED)` + `setDestination` | Hold / Passive off, the run speed mode RUN, one `MoveOrder` (`PgEngine.MoveTo`, a player-order walk: a conversation does not stop it) every 0.5 s; each re-issue allocates that one deferred command (every 0.5 s while walking, never per tick) | CAP 5 |
| `GetTickCount()` for the approach's dt and the variant seed (`GetTickCount() ^ (unsigned)(total hours x 60)`) | `PgClock.NowMs` (sim ms, frozen while paused) ^ `(uint)(World.GameHours x 60)`; the 0.5 s re-orders and the 20 s stall are game seconds; the cue times come from ArenaIngress's own clock (PgClock) | As every town glue; a variant still varies with the time |
| A deferred release (`deferNative`: the speaker unsafe to resume) gives its order receiver back at once and is not rethought until `TickReleases` resumes it | The external slot is given back at once, as the C++; the NPC stays held by its own owner `AnnouncerPending` (taken before the External hold goes back) until `TickReleases` resumes it (saved orders, speed, replan) or forgets it (gone, dead), so its AI does not take over while its saved Hold / Passive wait. An entry leaves the list before its restore, so a restore that throws is dropped (logged once, counted) instead of throwing at every tick; after a throw elsewhere an unsafe speaker is deferred the same way and a safe one restored; a pending hold whose entry is gone is given back by the backstop | Releasing the External hold would set `Ai.Replan` at once, the opposite of the C++'s no rethink |
| `AbandonWorldState` calls `ReleaseExternalOrders` for every owned speaker | No actor access: the world is going away, and TownAftercare's external table and the hold registry are emptied by their own world resets | The glue also forgets its host's world and cached buildings | As the limb shop's and the corpse cleanup's AbandonWorldState |
| Nothing in the C++ can throw | Begin, TickApproach, WaitingForPosition, BeginAnnouncement, TickAnnouncement, TickReleases and Release are caught by the glue (logged once per step, counted: F10's TownAnnouncer row, pg town announcer `failures`); after a throw every directed speaker gets its saved Hold / Passive / run speed back (each in its own try, a failed restore counted `restores`), then the cast is forgotten and every reservation the announcer took is given back, so HasAnnouncement answers false and ArenaIngress falls back to the fighters' countdown (as the C++'s FallBack); after every Release and every TickReleases any reservation that is not a current speaker's is given back | Port only: no reservation outlives its cast |
| A cast whose staging ended without `ClearPendingState` keeps its speakers | Port only backstop: TownArena.Tick's TickReleases releases a cast while ArenaIngress is not pending (logged once "announcer released without a staging", counted) | Every C++ path releases first (ClearPendingState, Finish); the backstop only catches a path the port might miss |
| The cast and its state are not saved | Not saved (holds are not saved either, CAP 19): after a load the staging is gone and so is the announcement | As the C++ |

## TownBookie (task 8.1a, the market, wagers and settlement: the sim half)

`Core/Glue/TownBookieLogic.cs` (every function of TownBookie.cpp that is not a widget call; fake-tested in `Tests/Glue/TownBookieLogicTests.cs`)
over Core `TownBookie` (MakeView, MakeResultView, Capture, the accepted text and fields, the settle messages) and `TownBettingPolicy` (used as they
are), glue `src/Town/TownBookie.cs`, the two windows' models and commands `src/Town/TownBookieUISim.cs` (the windows are task 8.1b), the trade
hook's bookie half `src/Game/ContextMenuHooks.cs` + `Core/Glue/ContextMenuHookLogic.cs` (BookieTradeRule, PendingTradeUi.QueueBookie). No new
by-name member (the S4 prefix's `Members.ConversationEnded` is task 5.3a's) and no saved field: the ticket rides the planned bout's
Market / WagerPending / WagerSide / Stake / Payout, the credit `LeaderboardStore.BookieCredit`, as the C++.

Kept as the C++ (not a divergence): the ingress hold for bets (`HoldForBets`, ArenaIngress.cpp:1641) never delays an NPC bout, because BeginEvent
calls `NpcAssembling` (elapsed = 20) before `ArenaIngress::Begin`; but :1641 has no match-kind guard, so a player challenge or a spar staged less
than 20 s (market time) after a market opened (for example a bout announced during aftercare, then a booking) is held there, in the C++ and here
alike (ArenaIngressLogic asks `BookieHoldForBets` the same way).

| Original | In this port | Why / effect |
|---|---|---|
| `showTradeWindow_hook` catches the bookie's TW_MONEY_TRADING (`first.isValid() && IsBookie(first)`, the same for second, exactly one of them) | The S4 prefix on `DialogueSystem.ConversationEffect` (task 5.3a), its TRADE on the choice "Show me the board before somebody dies.": record `111-Proving Grounds.mod` on a live character; with a valid player character on the other side it ends the conversation (`Conversation.Ended`) and queues the pending bookie, so no trade window is deferred; with none the game's trade runs (no line, as the C++ :193-194) | Meitou has no showTradeWindow; its dialogue TRADE opens only the money trade window ("Forced by Meitou"), so the TW_MONEY_TRADING test is always true |
| `TownBookieUI::Show` / `Refresh` / `Close` / `IsVisible` / `Destroy` and `TownBookieResultUI::Show` / `Close` / `IsVisible` / `Destroy` | The sim half keeps each window's visibility and publishes its view as a model (`pg.bookie`, `pg.bookie.result`): Show is "visible and published" (true), Destroy is "hidden"; the window half (task 8.1b) draws them. The result's "show failed" line (:431) is the window half's | The window framework (task 4.4): windows draw models, never the world |
| The window's buttons call SelectSide / ChangeStake / PlaceBet / Arrange / Close at once, enabled by MakeView's canSelect / canStake / canBet / canArrange and the stake buttons by `AdjustStake(stake, d) != stake` (TownBookieUI.cpp:404-407) | Each click is a command (`BookieSelectSide`, `BookieChangeStake`, `BookiePlaceBet`, `BookieArrange`, `BookieClose`) applied in the sim, run only while the betting window is open, with a request number above the model's LastSeq (the shared RecruitmentRequestGate numbers them), and while the button's enabled rule still holds on arrival (MakeView again); a stake click also needs the drawn stake, a bet the drawn side and stake; a close carries the drawn window's request serial (an older window's close never closes a newer one) | The window and the sim run on two threads (tasks 6.4a / 7.5's rule): a late or repeated click never acts on a state the player did not see |
| A click while the game is paused runs at once (MyGUI events) | It applies when the game runs (mod commands wait while paused, CAP 7.0); nothing is shown meanwhile: the C++ has no "pending" text for it (its "pending" strings are the ticket's state and "Pending Cats return must be collected before betting.") | "Forced by Meitou" / "Window framework" rows; the plan's "show the original's pending text" names a text that does not exist (task brief note 6) |
| `GetTickCount()` for the market's clock (`elapsed`, counted while `!ou->isPaused()`), the result's 1 s delay and the window's 250 ms refresh | `PgClock.NowMs`: sim milliseconds, frozen while paused (so the paused test is the clock itself); at a higher game speed the 20 s hold for bets, the 1 s and the 250 ms pass sooner in real time. The result's 1 s delay (:424) is wall-clock in the C++ (it is not paused-gated, so the result window opens while the game is paused); here it is sim time, so a result scheduled just before a pause opens once the game runs again | The general clock row ("One per-frame UI hook"); the hold is in game time as the C++ intends (scheduled bouts set it to 20 at staging) |
| `Wallet()` can be NULL (no player faction): no bet, money 0 in the header | Meitou's player always has a purse (`world.Player.Money`, a long) read as the C++ int (Core/Glue/TownChallengeWalletRule: clamped both ways); takeMoney = CanTake, PayCredit's guard `credit <= 0 \|\| money < 0 \|\| money > INT_MAX - credit` = CanCredit | As task 7.5's wallet |
| `Player(c)` scans `getAllPlayerCharacters()`; `AtBookie` scans it for a live one within 200 units in the bookie's town | `PgEngine.IsPlayer` (the character's own flag) for Player; `PgEngine.PlayerCharacters` for AtBookie; towns compared as `Towns.Key(CurrentTownOf)` (two "no town" keys equal, as two NULL pointers) | The same set (Meitou's player characters carry the flag) |
| Nothing in the C++ can throw | A fighter whose card or ability read throws in BeginNpcBout gets the "Unavailable fighter" card and ability 0 (so no market: "Team bout unavailable for betting: fighter data is incomplete."), counted and logged once ("bookie fighter read failed"); the other fighters are still read. A balance-log write that throws is caught, counted and logged once ("bookie market log failed"); the market goes on (F10's TownBookie row, pg town bookie `failures`) | Port only: one bad entry never stops the rest, and a log line never stops the market |
| TownBookie.cpp's state is file-level globals (one per process); AbandonWorldState resets all but `stake`, `elapsed` and `ability` (:405-418) and sets `message` to "NPC bouts only. Choose a side before combat." (:417), so those four carry over a load | The state is one TownBookieLogic per world, except those four, which live in one process-wide `TownBookieProcessState` (sim thread only) and so carry over a load the same way (the selected stake 500 is still 500 after a quickload; the status after a load reads :417's text, a fresh process :43's) | Meitou builds new systems per world; the three are kept as the C++ keeps them (controller decision, 8.1a) |
| A save while a wager is pending but no NPC bout is planned any more (it began staging) | The same: TownArena's CapturePlannedNpcBout answers `!HasPendingWager()` (TownArena.cpp:595), so the arena data is not written, and AbandonWorldState never moves money (:406-407): after loading that save the stake stays taken and no ticket comes back (fake-tested). In practice the save safety cancels a staging bout first (CancelForSave: Settle(-1) refunds) | Kept as the C++'s design (its comments: "Never move money during world teardown") |
| The windows close with a MyGUI callback while visible | A close (the Closed report's Value, or BookieClose) runs only for the window drawn under the current RequestSerial: a late close of the betting window never closes the result window Tick has just opened. Task 8.1b: both windows close only through the framework's Closed report or BookieClose with the drawn serial | Port only (two threads) |
| Escape (ArenaUI.cpp:2984-2986) and the close buttons call TownBookie::Close | The window's Closed report (task 4.4) and the close command call the same Close | The Escape key is the window framework's |

## Bookie windows, window half (task 8.1b)

`src/Ui/TownBookieUI.cs` (TownBookieScreen, `pg.bookie`) and `src/Ui/TownBookieResultUI.cs` (TownBookieResultScreen, `pg.bookie.result`), the
pixel arithmetic in `Core/Glue/TownBookieUILayout.cs` and `Core/Glue/TownBookieResultUILayout.cs` (tested in `Tests/Glue/TownBookieUILayoutTests.cs`),
the clicks numbered by `Core/Glue/RecruitmentRequestGate.cs`. Every widget keeps the C++'s name (EnsureWindow / CreateCard name them all).

| Original | In this port | Why / effect |
|---|---|---|
| Both windows are made once (EnsureWindow) with four cards a side and shown / hidden; Refresh runs every 250 ms and lays out each time; the result window lays out once in Show | One XML layout per window, one shape (never Replaced); the window lays out once per model, view or font and binds that plan every frame | Window and sim threads are separate (overview §9); same picture |
| `bet->getFontHeight()` (Body, Spacing) and `NativeUI::RowHeight(bet / arrange / closeButton, 0)` use the buttons' font | The standard text's font height and the captions' standard-text heights | As every window since 4.5b (one measure; Meitou has no per-widget font height) |
| Layout sets the betting window to 88% x 90% of the view only when the view size changes; the result window is fitted to its content in Show | The window rectangle is bound every frame from the layout (the framework keeps a player's drag offset, as every window); the result window is fitted again when the view or font changes while it is open | As the other windows |
| The result window's Layout measures the title's and payout's `getTextSize().width` at the width each label had before (100 px at creation, then the previous Show's) | The same: the window keeps the widths of the last layout (100 again at a new world: Destroy) | Faithful; noted because it is state outside the model |
| Select / Stake / Bet / Arrange act inside the click | Their commands carry the drawn model's values (BookieSelectSide the side; BookieChangeStake the button's own delta and the drawn stake; BookiePlaceBet the drawn side and stake; BookieArrange) and a number from `RecruitmentRequestGate.Send`: a second click is not sent while the first is unanswered; the sim re-checks each against the button's enabled rule (task 8.1a) | A click is judged against what the player saw; a click on a stake drawn before the last one's answer is refused, not applied twice |
| The result window's Close (CloseClicked) and both windows' title-bar close / Escape call TownBookie::Close | Close sends BookieClose with the RequestSerial of the model the window was drawn with; the title-bar close and Escape are the framework's Closed report, which carries that serial too; neither ever uses the current serial | A late close of the betting window must not close the result window Tick has just opened (8.1a quality review) |
| The wheel scrolls the roster by 3 body heights a notch (Wheel :131-137, BindWheel on every card part) | Meitou's scroll view wheel (50 px a notch); the offset is clamped after each layout as Layout :355 clamps it, and 0 again at a new world | As every window (task 4.5b) |
| The fighter tip: eventToolTip from MyGUI's ToolTipManager (0.5 s rest), an overlapped background raised with upLayerItem | The pointer polled each bind with `TooltipTimer` (the same 0.5 s rule); sized and placed by the town window's ports of the same arithmetic (`TownArenaUILayout.FighterTip` / `PlaceTip`: PortraitTip and PlaceTip are identical in the two C++ files); the background is the client's last child | As the town window (task 7.6) |
| `setTextColour` on the timer and stake value EditBoxes (.95/.72/.22), on the result title, payout and headings | Written in the XML (the betting window) and also bound on the EditBox template's caption part (`ScreenBinding.EditTextColour`) | Meitou draws an EditBox's caption on the template's client part (task 7.6 row) |
| BindCard shows a portrait for every card with a valid actor (PortraitManager) | Hidden when no portrait cell is free; the card's placeholder ("Portrait unavailable" / the result's "?") shows instead, as the C++ shows it without a portrait | No portrait API in Meitou (CAP 7.4, 7.6's path) |
| The roster's vertical bar: MyGUI shows it only while the canvas overflows | The bar the layout settled on is bound as VisibleVScroll each frame | Meitou keeps both bars for a canvas exactly the size of the bar-less view (task 7.6 row) |
| `try { WhiteSkin accent } catch (...) { accent = NULL; }`: decoration optional | The accents are always made (WhiteSkin is a Kenshi skin; PgCheck ui checks every skin) | Nothing can throw in an XML layout |
| A failed EnsureWindow / Refresh logs "Proving Grounds: bookie UI creation failed: <what>" / "... refresh failed: <what>" and Destroy; the result window "Proving Grounds: wager results UI creation failed: <what>", then TownBookie.cpp:431 "Proving Grounds: bookie wager result show failed" | The framework's Failed hook logs the same lines; the framework reports the window closed when it was asked open | As the town window; the sim half has already logged "bookie wager result shown" (it cannot know the window failed) |

Kept as the C++ (not a divergence): the result window's Layout caps the window at 90% of the view and has no scroll, so a result with three or four
cards a side (two card rows) is taller than the window at small views: at 1600 x 900 (font 21) a 2v3 result wants a client of 944 px (2 x 42 title
and context, 614 teams, 105 payout, 41 Close, 100 gaps) in an 810 px window, and the Close button lies below it (seen live, town-bookie run 3: the
refund's Close was not drawn); the title-bar close and Escape still close it.

The live pictures `_shots/provinggrounds/town-bookie.png`, `town-bookie-placed.png` and `town-bookie-result.png` (town-bookie, 1600 x 900, font
height 21) compared with ws-13 (2246 x 1288) and ws-07 (1253 x 1120), both Kenshi's dark theme and an older build. Betting window, the same
structure: the title, the header box "BETTING OPEN | 5000 Cats | NPC bout 1v2" and the gold timer "NEXT BOUT 11:49 | STARTS IN 0h 52m" side by
side, the roster with "TEAM A | 1.75x return" / "TEAM B | 1.85x return" each above its cards, the red / blue accent rule over each card, name and
style, the portrait with "Unrated | 0 W / 0 L" beside it, "VS" between the teams, Select Team A / B, the six stake buttons around the gold
"STAKE: 500 CATS", the slip and status boxes, Place bet and Arrange next NPC bout. What differs: Meitou's parchment skin on the roster and text
boxes (the client itself stays dark); the window is 88% x 90% of the view (ws-13 is nearly the whole screen: the older build); Team B's two cards
stand one under the other (at 1600 x 900 a team column is 622 px (with the bar), under the 35 bodies = 735 px the C++ needs for two cards a row; ws-13's wider
view puts them side by side) so the roster scrolls; the selected side reads "TEAM A [SELECTED]" and its button "TEAM A SELECTED" (ws-13 shows the
unselected state, the C++'s Refresh :412-416); the C++'s heading separator is two spaces each side ("  |  "), drawn narrow by the font as in
ws-13. After the bet the slip "ACCEPTED: ..." wraps to two lines and the rows above move up (Wrap, Layout), the selected side button keeps its
caption with every button disabled. Result window: the same as ws-07: the green "WAGER WON" between two green rails, the context line, "VICTOR -
TEAM A [YOUR PICK]" gold and "DEFEATED - TEAM B" red over their rules, square portraits framed gold / red with name and style under them, the green
"STAKE 500 CATS RETURNED 875 CATS / NET WIN +375 CATS" and CLOSE centred below. What differs: the team, fighters and sums (ws-07 is a 3v4 win for
Team B); CLOSE in Meitou's Kenshi_Button1 face (grey caption on dark); the cards' names and styles and the context line are drawn dim grey-brown on the
dark client where ws-07 shows near-white: the C++ gives those labels no colour (CreateCard :106-108, EnsureWindow :131), so they take the skin's
default text colour, which in Meitou's palette is the dark ink made for its parchment boxes (the betting window's labels sit on parchment and read
well), not Kenshi's light text (measured: no pixel of the "Meow" name or the context line in town-bookie-result.png is brighter than the
client behind it, (45, 40, 37), so the text is darker than the background, against (169, 169, 169) for a name in ws-07; that the cause is
Meitou's default ink is INFERRED, the palette source was not traced); one victor card on the left (a 1v2), so the left column's second slot is
empty.

The live pictures `_shots/provinggrounds/town-full-bookie.png`, `town-full-result.png` (task 8.8, town-full, a 1v2 bout, 500 on Team A) match
`town-bookie.png` / `town-bookie-result.png` above in every point listed there (ws-13 / ws-07). `town-full-crowd.png` (the game paused on the
crowd's first fight line, "Stay on them, Meow!", the camera on the speaker) beside ws-15: the same Scratch arena with the audience on the Arena
Pillows and the line drawn over the speaker's head. What differs: ws-15 shows the town's own ambient lines ("A caravan came through with no
goods ...", "Saw the bookie buy a fighter a drink ..."), which are none of TownSpectators' lines (Core.TownSpectators.ChatterLine /
ResultChatterLine: not in the C++ plugin, so not the port's) and residents walking in, not seated; the bubble: Meitou's bark (CAP 16) draws the
text without ws-15's dark band behind it, one line at a time per speaker (2 voices a cycle, so at most two bubbles from the crowd at once).

## Test-only commands

The `pg` dev-console command itself is the port's (spike S14; the original had only its F8 debug menu). Every verb answers one JSON
line (task 2.7, `Core/Glue/PgConsoleAnswer.cs`): `{"verb":..,"ok":true,..}`, or `{"verb":..,"ok":false,"error":..}` printed as a console
error. Verbs that need the world are queued for the next tick and answer in log.txt (PG-PROBE, PG-CENSUS, PG-IDENTITY, `pg <verb> at tick`).
Diagnostic verbs, always on: `pg help`, `pg status` (with the F10 status row as `row`), `pg census` (task 0.6), `pg probe [<id>]` (task 2.2),
`pg probe-config` (task 2.6), `pg spikes on|off` (arms the rest; off also makes the next tick forget the spike verbs' holds, spar and carry job, as every new world does). Test-only verbs, armed only by `pg spikes on` (off by default), for the
scripted live runs (the M0 spike verb flags and the spike list keep their M0 text answers; spike S6/S7's win / portrait were replaced by `pg window test` in task 4.4):

| Verb | What it does | Task |
|---|---|---|
| `pg identity-dupes`, `pg identity create <id>` | log fighter-identity token repeats; GetOrCreate for one character | 2.3 |
| `pg give-rating <id> <n>` | the C++ debug menu's rating setter (DebugMenu.cpp:398-430 -> LeaderboardStore::SetRating, :369-395) as a console verb: refused while the arena is blocked or without an identity, clamped to 0..9999 (a float), answered with the debug menu's texts ("Rating updated for the active world." / "Could not save rating (load a game first).") | 2.4 |
| `pg give-marks <id> <n>` | the C++ debug menu's marks setter (DebugMenu.cpp:432-445 -> LeaderboardStore::SetMarks, :506-532; since task 5.1 src/Game/LeaderboardStore.cs, the debug menu's own setter) as a console verb: refused while blocked or without an identity, clamped to 0..9999 (an int), the debug menu's texts | 2.4 |
| `pg busy [on\|off]` | a stand-in match for the save safety: while on the arena counts as busy (a manual save is refused once, the stand-in match is cancelled by `SparSession.AbortForSave`, and the save is re-requested and written; an autosave waits); off releases a waiting autosave. Replaces the spike's `pg busy refuse-once\|delay` | 2.5 |
| `pg setting <key> <value>` | sets one settings-page row (`<key>` = its property name, e.g. `DebugMenuKey V`) through Meitou's `ModSetting.Set`, which raises the group's Changed as the page's row does (Mods/ModSettings.cs:162-176, Settings/ModSettingsRows.cs:109); not kept in meitou.user.json (the page's own bookkeeping). Frame thread, no world access; an at-once row is applied on the next tick (`pg probe-config` then shows it). For the live run: the agent's `ui-set` moves sliders only (AgentChannel.cs:1058-1076) | final review I1 (2.6) |
| `pg spar <avb\|1v1\|ffa> <idA...> [vs <idB...>]`, `pg stop` | `pg spar` = SparSession::StartMatch as the original's Arena window calls it after its ingress (ArenaUI.cpp:2388-2430, ArenaIngress.cpp:942), without the ingress (formation, countdown: task 4.3): `avb` = AvB (TeamAvB), `1v1` = Teams 1v1 (Teams1v1: one of each team fights, the rest wait on the bench), `ffa` = Last Standing; the team modes list team A then team B after `vs` (TeamA / TeamB), Last Standing the pool (TeamNone); every StartMatch refusal answers with its C++ status text. `pg stop` = the window's Stop (ArenaUI.cpp:2476, `Stop(StopManual)`): outcome Stopped, results, no rating. Answers PG-SPAR JSON lines in log.txt: `start` (ok, the C++ status, the fighters' orders before and after the stance, bench and active flags), `status` (a status change while a match runs: a Teams 1v1 promotion), `result` (outcome, podium, per fighter the rating before/after/delta and the Marks computed, logged where the C++ schedules the results window), `end` (the final status, the fighters' state after the disengage), `stop` (pg stop's answer). Replaces 3.1's `pg fight` / `pg unfight` stand-in; task 3.5 keeps both verbs | 3.3 |
| `pg ring at <x> <y> <z> <arena\|small\|banner> [scale <f>]`, `pg ring around <id...> <kind> [scale <f>]`, `pg ring off`, `pg ring status`, `pg ring push <id> <distance> [<bearing>]`, `pg ring sample [near <id>] <radius...>` | the ring guard's site by hand (since task 4.3b an ingress also sets it with `SetArena` of the staged site; the verb stays test-only): `at` a fixed point, `around` the mean position of those characters, with the site's kind (`arena` x1.0, `small` x0.6, `banner` no ring, as RingScaleForSite); `scale <f>` replaces the kind's scale (no C++ counterpart: a small ring for a live check); `off` = no site; `status` the site, the radii in force, the corrections made (`returned`, `steppedIn`, `downedLeft`) and each participant's 2D distance from the centre; `push` puts a character at that distance from the centre on its own bearing (or the bearing given in degrees from +x toward +z, when the guard's return spot on it is walkable too) with the guard's own write (grounded at the ring's floor: the centre's height as the floor hint, then, where nothing stands there, the character's own, the guard's hint (task 4.8); walkable spots only, Place keeping its task), so a check can watch the guard bring it back; with a bearing the return spot is required only when the push lands in the near band or outside (where the guard acts; task 4.8, `Core/Glue/RingPushRule.cs`), and it is checked with `TryStand` from the pushed spot's ground height, as the guard's own hint (before task 4.8: `IsWalkable` at the centre's height). ring.sh's pushes keep the near-band rule (all of them land in the near band or outside); the return spot is checked from the pushed height, as the guard's own hint, and the push may fall back to the character's own height where nothing stands at the centre's (so it is not byte-exact with before task 4.8; ring scenario 20 PASS 0 FAIL with this code, 2026-11-15 03:03-03:04 UTC); refused for a downed or carried body, a distance over twice the out radius (1000 while the ring is inert) and an unwalkable spot. `status` also gives the gate stamps (`nearAt`, `outAt`, PgClock ms) and `now`; the counts (`returned`, `steppedIn`, `downedLeft`, `unwalkableLeft`) start again at every `SetArena`. `sample` (task 4.8, a measurement, moves nobody): at each radius (at most 16) on 36 bearings (every 10 degrees) `IsWalkable` and `TryStand` with the centre's height as the floor hint and, with `near <id>`, with that character's; a failed stand's height and a non-finite one are `null`. Answers one PG-RING JSON line in log.txt | 3.4, 4.8 |
| `pg watch <id...>`, `pg watch off` | up to 8 characters watched; once per tick (the end of the system's serial step) each one's attack target (CombatSystem.TargetOf, last tick), down / dead, in combat and running task (row, source, start tick: CharacterCold.Running) are read, and a PG-WATCH JSON line is written only when that state changed (never per tick); a new world forgets the watch. The gate scenarios time the fight start, the KO, the retarget after a KO and "nobody targets anyone after the end / stop / load" by these ticks. Task 3.5 also adds test-output fields: `pg probe` gives `tick`, `running` (row, source, target, tick), `bounty` (summed over factions), `pendingCrime`, `engaged` (the bench hold) and `relations` (FactionRelations.Changes, the town's relation to the player both ways); the PG-SPAR `end` and `status` lines give `scanTick` / `prevScanTick` (KOWatcher's last two scans: a KO seen at scanTick happened after prevScanTick); `pg stop`'s ok answer is a PG-SPAR `stop` line too | 3.5 |

| `pg perf [on\|off\|reset]` | (task 9.2a) the cost-per-tick instrument (Core/Glue/TickCost.cs, src/Engine/PgPerf.cs; not in the original): it ships in the Release build, off by default (off = one volatile bool read per measured site, nothing else; on = `Stopwatch.GetTimestamp()` at the start and end, no allocation). `on` / `off` switch it for the process, `reset` zeroes the patch counters at once and empties the tick windows on the next measured tick. Every form answers at once on the frame thread (no world access): `on`, `tick` and `inputs` (SlowWorld's and Inputs' average and maximum in ms over the last 600 measured ticks, and how many; from the last 0.20 s publish; null when nothing was measured in this world) and `patches` (each Harmony patch method of PgPatches.All(), 31 slots: calls, total, average and maximum in ms since the last reset; updated with Interlocked, as some run on the parallel AI threads). While on, the F10 row "Proving Grounds: status" ends with `; tick avg <x> ms max <y> ms (<n>)` (CMath.FormatF, 3 decimals); off, the row is as before, but only from the next publish: the piece lingers after `pg perf off` until the sim thread publishes again (0.20 s of game time, so it stays while the game is paused). The `reset` answer's `tick` / `inputs` still show the old windows: they are emptied on the next measured serial step and published after it. PgCheck hooks checks the slot table against PgPatches.All() (count, each slot's name, and the slot constant each patch method passes to PgPerf.Measure). Task 9.2b adds `steps` to the answer: each SlowWorld step (Towns.Refresh, FighterIdentity.Refresh, Spikes.Tick, the two FightStarter steps, the 20 frame-loop steps in order, WorldLifecycle.AfterFrame, CombatHooks.AfterSerialStep, PgWatch.Tick, Publish) with calls, total, average and maximum since the last reset (one timestamp per step while on; null before the world's first step), and while on a SlowWorld of 2 ms or more writes one `PG-PERF {"event":"spike",...}` line to Meitou's log: its tick and ms, its steps of 0.25 ms or more, the garbage collections (gen 0/1/2) and the sim thread's JIT time and method count in it, and the time spent writing test-only lines (`testLineMs`: PG-INGRESS / PG-PRISONER / PG-TOWN say lines, armed only) and log-file lines (`fileLineMs`, PgLogSink) in it (process-wide), and the sim thread's own CPU time in it (`cpuMs`: QueryThreadCycleTime cycles over a rate measured once by a 2 ms busy wait; null off Windows; far under `ms` = the thread was descheduled). The measurement is `tools/scenarios/perf.sh` (a first bout, then a steady-state second bout in the same session after the JIT warm-up line) | 9.2a, 9.2b |
`pg spar` / `pg stop` stay test-only for M3 (refused until `pg spikes on`): M4 replaces the start with the Registry walk-up and the Arena
window (ingress, task 4.3), and the stop with the window's Stop button. `pg ring` stays test-only after task 4.3b (an ingress now sets the
ring site from the staged building; `pg ring push` / `status` remain the ring guard's live check).

Every verb the dispatcher (`src/Engine/PgConsole.cs`, and `TownArena.Command` for the `pg town` words) accepts has a row or a sentence above (checked task 9.3b). One verb the milestone plans named was never built: `pg unlock-blueprints` (M5; the scenarios get blueprints through the Rewards purchases, `pg reward <id> licence ...` and `pg reward-item`).

| K4 | Quote and selection use different strength models: opponents are chosen on stats only (MMR influence 0) but priced with MMR influence 1.0, so a high-MMR opponent prices as stronger than the band that picked it | `TownMatchmakingPolicy.h:253`; `TownArena.cpp:740-741` | TW §5 item 4 |
| K5 | The solo named duel has no cap: the comment promises rejection "when the unique exceeds the safety cap", but the code returns the duel whenever the score is positive | `TownMatchmakingPolicy.h:282-284`, `:285-300` | TW §5 item 5 |

| K8 | The bookie's credit add has no guard: `Settle` does `LeaderboardStore::GetBookieCredit() += credit` on an int (only RefundUnrestoredWager checks `INT_MAX - credit`), which wraps on MSVC / x86. A wrapped (negative) credit is never paid (PayCredit needs `credit > 0`) and Wager refuses every later bet for good (`GetBookieCredit() != 0`). Reachable only with an unpaid credit near INT_MAX (the money itself near INT_MAX). The port wraps the same way (`unchecked`, Core/Glue/TownBookieLogic.cs Settle; fake-tested) | `TownBookie.cpp:389`, `:90`, `:100` | controller decision, task 8.1a quality review |

The lever ships off (`showcasePercent` 0), so shipped play is unaffected | `TownMatchmakingPolicy.h:453`, `:464-465`, `:514`, `:536-537` | not in the inventory (found in task 1.10; `Tests/Golden/matchmaking.json` tune rows 1 and 3) |

The port asserts the shipped result | `tests/TownChallengePolicyTests.cpp:69-94`; `TownMatchmakingPolicy.h:301-315` | not in the inventory (found in task 1.10) |

Check Arena Marks before saving." before `ForgetRosterPrisoner`, so the new squad member is still a roster prisoner until the next discovery. Nothing is left reserved: `Accept` is false only when the reservation is no longer pending (`ArenaLedger::Pending`; `EndWorld` clears `reserved`, ArenaLedger.h:32-36, :101-120), so it never blocks a later purchase. In the other modes a KO'd fighter is eliminated at once and Ringside Aid treats him. The port keeps both rules (`Core/ArenaMedical.cs`, KOWatcher in `src/Game/KOWatcher.cs`) | `KOWatcher.cpp:117-124`; `ArenaMedicalPolicy.cpp:11-15` | PA §5 last row (INFERRED there; the two code lines read as stated) |

## Number formatting for glue

Glue and UI files (M2-M8) that print a float or double must use the Core helpers, never .NET "F"/"G"/"R", interpolation of a double, or
`Math.Round`: `CMath.FormatF(value, decimals)` for `%.Nf` (and `std::fixed << std::setprecision(N)`), `CMath.FormatG(value, precision)` for
`%g` / `%.Ng` (and an `ostream << double`, default precision 6), `Msvcr100Format.E` for `%e`. A float argument is passed as is (C promotes
it to double, the C# call does the same, exactly). Flags Core has no helper for: `%+.1f` = `(double.IsNegative(v) ? "" : "+") +
CMath.FormatF(v, 1)` (msvcr100 prints `+0.0` for 0 and `-0.0` for -0 and for -0.04), `%%` is a literal `%`. Integers (`%d %u %x %02d`)
print the same in every runtime; MSVC `long` is 32-bit.

Speed: one call costs about 1.6 µs and 570-730 bytes of garbage (the 80-bit emulation, measured by the M1.14 quality review). UI code
caches the formatted text and formats again only when the value changes; per-frame formatting of an unchanged number is not allowed.
If M9's speed check (task 9.2) shows the formatter in a profile, the later option is to decode the power-of-ten table entries once at
start and keep the mantissas in `Span`/`UInt128` locals instead of `ushort[]` (same algorithm, no allocation per multiply).

Upstream sites (`grep -o '%[-+ 0#]*[0-9]*\(\.[0-9*]*\)\?[fgeEG]'` over `plugin/src`, a letter may follow the conversion as in `%.2fx`; plus the iostream ones), by file:

| File (milestone) | Sites |
|---|---|
| ArenaIngress.cpp (M4) | :205 `%.1f %.0f`, :708 and :1703 `%.1f %.2f` (logs) |
| ArenaRingGuard.cpp (M3) | :126, :136 `%.0f %.2f` (logs) |
| ArenaUI.cpp (M4) | :564 `%.0f` x4 (combat stats), :1659, :1669 `~%.0f combat` |
| DebugMenu.cpp (M4) | :314 `%.0f%%` (reduced MMR influence x 100) |
| PGSettingsUI.cpp (M2) | :219, :230, :232-234 `%.2f` (setting fields); :332 `strtod` (see below) |
| PrisonerRecruitmentUI.cpp (M6) | :266 `Combat %.0f` |
| ResultsUI.cpp (M4) | :529, :548 `%.0f` x3; :531 `RATING %.1f (%+.1f)` |
| RewardDelivery.cpp (M5) | :411 `%.3f` (log) |
| SparSession.cpp (M3) | :252 `%.1f` x3 (log) |
| TownAftercare.cpp (M8) | 21 sprintf_s logs (:248-1288), `%.0f %.1f %.3f %.4f %.5f` |
| TownArenaUI.cpp (M7) | :540, :544 `%.0f` x5/x6 (compact stats); :551 `%.2fx` (odds); :557 `%.2fx` x4 (marks breakdown) |
| TownCorpseCleanup.cpp (M8) | :260 `%.1f` x3 (log) |
| TownSpectators.cpp (M8) | :390 `%.1f` x2 (log) |
| TownMatchmakingRuntime.cpp (M7) | :159 `ostream << std::setprecision(17)` (the doubles written after it are `%.17g`) |

Already in Core: ArenaCombatProfile.cpp:104 (iostream fixed 0), BalanceTuning.cpp, CombatBalanceLog.cpp:48, PGConfig.cpp, TownArena.cpp :341
:1059 :1219 :1644, TownBookie.cpp :125 :174, TownDiagnosticRun.cpp :66 :137, TownChallengePolicy.h:261-263, ArenaSnapshotJson.cpp:143-147.

### strtod (PGSettingsUI, M2)

`PGSettingsUI.cpp:327-348` (`ParseNumberText`) reads a typed setting with msvcr100 `strtod`: whole text, trailing `isspace` allowed,
a non-finite result kept only with `errno == ERANGE`. Measured 2026-11-12 (`MeitouMods/_strtodprobe`, `strtod_report.md`; copy in
`proving-grounds-research/msvcr100-printf/`) against .NET `double.TryParse(text, NumberStyles.Float, InvariantCulture)`:

- equal: short decimals (`1`, `1.5`, `.5`, `5.`, `-0`, `+3`, `00012`), exponents (`1e3`, `1E+3`, `1e0010`), leading/trailing spaces and tabs,
  overflow (`1e400` -> infinity, ERANGE: accepted as infinity by ParseNumberText), the largest double, rejected junk (`abc`, `1,5`, `1e`,
  `1.2.3`, empty, blank); hex is not parsed by msvcr100 (`0x1A` stops after the `0`: rejected) nor by .NET; `inf`, `INF`, `-inf`,
  `nan(123)`, `1.#INF`, `1.#IND` are rejected by both.
- differ: (1) `infinity`, `nan`, `NAN`: msvcr100 rejects, .NET accepts (infinity / NaN); (2) `d`/`D` is an exponent letter for
  msvcr100 (`1d5` = 100000, `1D-2` = 0.01, `-2d+1` = -20), .NET rejects; (3) msvcr100 is not correctly rounded: 126 of 1,000,000 random
  texts give a different double, all large magnitudes (`8349623e13` -> 8.349622999999999e19; 52 of 200,000 typed-style decimals with an
  exponent, 39 of 400,000 G17/R round trips of random doubles, 35 of 100,000 long mantissas, 0 of 300,000 G17 texts of values below 1e7);
  `9007199254740993.0000000001` -> ...992 (.NET ...994); (4) the subnormal halfway `2.4703282292062328e-324` -> 0 with ERANGE (.NET 5e-324).
  The glue port of ParseNumberText needs its own strtod rules for (1) and (2) at least; (3) and (4) matter only for values far outside
  every setting's range.
- Core: no Core function uses strtod. The challenge codec's `>> double` (TownChallengePolicy.h, `Core/TownChallengePolicy.Codec.cs`
  `Double`) follows libstdc++'s field collection; the shipped original reads it with msvcp100's `num_get`, which is not measured here.
  Values the codec writes (game hours below 2.4e7, `%.17g`) read back to the same double in both (0 of 300,000 differ).

## Strings with no counterpart (task 9.3a, G2)

These pieces of the original's player-visible text have no code path in the port. Each line starts with `string:` (the form the strings gate reads).

### ArenaNativePath.h

The save-folder path conversion belongs to the sidecar file save path; Meitou's own save carries the arena data (overview §1 table, "Forced by Meitou" persistence rows), so there is no native path to resolve or decode.

string: Cannot decode native save path as UTF-8 or Windows ANSI — no counterpart (reason in the sentence above)

string: Resolving save directory for — no counterpart (reason in the sentence above)

string: ' failed (Win32 error  — no counterpart (reason in the sentence above)

### ArenaNativePersistence.cpp

The native save/load hooks, the emergency-save search and the sidecar's generation matching are replaced by the arena data stored inside the Meitou save (`IModSaveData`, task 2.4; overview §1 table and the persistence rows of "Forced by Meitou"); the port's own texts for the failures that remain are in `Core/Glue/ArenaWorldState.cs`.

## Completeness check (task 9.3b)

Done for G1 and the plan's "divergences.md is complete" line. Nothing below changes behaviour; it points each item to where it is written.

### Overview §1 table (the 10 known differences): what, why, effect on play

| # | Difference | Where above | Effect on play |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 37 KenshiLib hooks replaced by Meitou API + Harmony patches | "Forced by Meitou" row 1; each hook's own rows (CombatHooks, ContextMenuHooks, TownAftercare 8.4a ...) | The same rules fire at the same moments; the places where a hook could not be matched are the rows named there (soft-miss net not built, no `isEnemy` hooks, no `checkOrders` hook). |
| 2 | Real-time clock replaced by a simulation clock | row 2 | Identical at 1x speed; at 2x / 5x the timers (ring guard, countdowns, bookie, aftercare) follow game time; nothing runs while paused. |
| 3 | Sidecar files replaced by Meitou mod data in the save | rows 3, 5-9 and "Strings with no counterpart" | Saves and loads can no longer mismatch a sidecar; RE_Kenshi sidecars are not imported (D7); the sidecar texts are not shown. |
| 4 | Fighter UUID in the character's save state replaced by PortraitSerial + RecordId | row 4 | Same career across squad moves, save and load; two live characters with one token trip the duplicate guard where the UUIDs would not. |
| 5 | F8 debug menu key replaced by the "Debug menu key" setting | "Forced by Meitou" rows "F8 toggles ..." and "F8 debug menu shows ..." (the key list Off / O / N / V); "Marks HUD and debug menu (task 4.6b)" | The debug menu is off by default as before; the player picks a key (Off, O, N, V) instead of F8, which Meitou uses for screenshots. |
| 6 | "PG CONFIG" button replaced by a Meitou mod settings group | "Forced by Meitou" row "PG CONFIG button ..." and "Settings page strings not reproduced (task 2.6)" | Same 19 settings, in Esc > Settings > Mods; the original's window captions and edit-box flow are Meitou's page. |
| 7 | Jobs off by key simulation replaced by Meitou's job switch | row "Jobs off by selecting the character ..." | The selection does not change and no click sound plays; the jobs stay off during the bout and come back at the end, as before. |
| 8 | Cloak record 72 / 89 choice replaced by 89 always | row "Cloak record 72 ..." and the Rewards row "The cloak is 72 ..." | The Warlord cloak is the plain cloak (record 89), as in the original without the "Extra Inventory Sections" plugin. |
| 9 | Windows registered non-modal | row "Kenshi windows do not pause the game" | Opening a window does not pause the game, as in the original. |
| 10 | Mod commands queued while paused | row "Mod commands apply at once" | A click made while paused takes effect when the game runs again (the window shows the old state until then). |

| S14 `pg` console command | a port-only command, test verbs armed by `pg spikes on` | "Test-only commands" |

| spikes-research "up-front answers" (how a scripted run calls mod code; `--mods-cfg`) | test harness only, no effect on play | not a divergence |

ws-07 wager result, ws-08 / 09 / 10 rewards, ws-11 results, ws-12 town board, ws-13 betting; ws-02 to ws-05, ws-14, ws-15 are world views
and ws-01 an animation, no window). Only what is new or changed since the comparisons above (tasks 4.6a, 4.8, 5.3b, 6.4b, 7.6, 8.1b) is
written out; everything else is the palette, font size, separator and space-run rows already listed there.

FEEDBACK over the close button: known (4.5b row, line 254) |

Every listed window has a picture today. Not at 1920 x 1080: the bookie windows, the crowd and the settings page (their scenarios run at
1600 x 900). New difference: one (the Results header colour).

Fixed after the gate: the Results header colour. `ResultsUI.cs` Bind now calls `ScreenBinding.EditTextColour(N.Header, HeaderText)` (gold `RankColour(1)`, `ResultsUI.cpp:394`) on the widget and its template caption part, as the registry and bookie windows do; `PgCheck ui` (`Check/UiCheck.cs` `ResultsBoundFields`) checks it bound on both. The other Results EditBoxes (context, empty, names, stats) set no colour in the C++, and the Leaderboard has no coloured EditBox (its gold and rank colours are on TextBoxes), so it needed no change.

Workshop preview candidates (nothing copied or edited): `arena-ingress-avb-fight.png` (a 3v3 bout in the Scratch arena, the stands and ring
wall in view; but empty stands and unarmed test fighters), `arena-full-avb-ready.png` (the Arena window at 1920 x 1080 with both teams
filled: the mod's main window at its best), `town-full-crowd.png` (the only picture with the audience on the stands and a fight, but the game's
"PAUS..." banner is across the top, so only as a crop). None shows an armed bout with a crowd unobstructed: a dedicated shot (town bout,
unpaused, camera on the ring) would make the best preview.
